#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_predictive_step_updates_priors() {
let mut model = GenerativeModel::new();
let obs = Observation {
extero: [q88_from_i32(5), 0, 0, 0],
proprio: [0; N_PROPRIO],
};

let prior_before = model.extero_priors[0];
let (_, _) = predictive_step(&mut model, &obs);
let prior_after = model.extero_priors[0];

// Prior should move toward observation
assert!(prior_after > prior_before, "prior should update toward observation");
}

#[test]
fn test_predictive_convergence() {
let mut model = GenerativeModel::new();
let obs = Observation {
extero: [q88_from_i32(5), q88_from_i32(3), 0, 0],
proprio: [q88_from_i32(2), 0, 0, 0],
};

// Run 100 steps
for _ in 0..100 {
let (_, _) = predictive_step(&mut model, &obs);
}

// Priors should converge close to observation values
let diff0 = (model.extero_priors[0] - obs.extero[0]).abs();
let diff1 = (model.extero_priors[1] - obs.extero[1]).abs();

assert!(diff0 < q88_from_i32(1), "prior 0 should converge: diff={}", diff0);
assert!(diff1 < q88_from_i32(1), "prior 1 should converge: diff={}", diff1);
}

#[test]
fn test_free_energy_non_negative() {
let mut model = GenerativeModel::new();
let obs = Observation {
extero: [q88_from_i32(10), 0, 0, 0],
proprio: [0; N_PROPRIO],
};

let (_, _) = predictive_step(&mut model, &obs);

// Free energy should be positive (it's a sum of squared errors)
assert!(model.free_energy >= 0, "free energy should be non-negative: {}", model.free_energy);
}

#[test]
fn test_error_to_somatic_mapping() {
let errors = [
q88_from_i32(1), q88_from_i32(2), q88_from_i32(3), q88_from_i32(4),
q88_from_i32(5), q88_from_i32(6), q88_from_i32(7), q88_from_i32(8),
 ];
let field = errors_to_somatic_input(&errors);

// Extero errors → channels 0-3
assert_eq!(field[0], errors[0]);
assert_eq!(field[1], errors[1]);

// Proprio errors → channels 4-7
assert_eq!(field[4], errors[4]);
assert_eq!(field[5], errors[5]);

// Aggregates → channels 8-9, should be non-zero
assert!(field[8] > 0, "mean error magnitude should be positive");
}

#[test]
fn test_action_reduces_error() {
let mut model = GenerativeModel::new();
let obs = Observation {
extero: [0; N_EXTERO],
proprio: [q88_from_i32(5), 0, 0, 0], // strong proprio error
};

let (action, errors) = predictive_step(&mut model, &obs);

// Action should be non-zero when there are errors
let action_nonzero = action.motor.iter().any(|&m| m != 0);
let errors_nonzero = errors.iter().any(|&e| e != 0);

assert!(errors_nonzero, "should have prediction errors");
assert!(action_nonzero, "should produce action");
}
}

=== FILE: src/eps/morphology.rs ===

//! EPS Morphology — Body Shape as Computational Constraint
//!
//! The morphology (body plan) causally constrains the predictive layer.
//! An eyeless morphology (extero_sensitivity = 0) literally cannot see
//! the corresponding channel, regardless of what the world provides.
//!
//! This is Levin's thesis in minimal form: the body is not merely
//! controlled by the mind — it IS a computation. Shape constrains
//! what can be perceived and what can be done.

use crate::eps::predictive::{GenerativeModel, N_EXTERO, N_PROPRIO, Observation, Action};

/// A body plan that causally constrains prediction and action.
///
/// extero_sensitivity: per-channel scaling [0, 1.0] (Q8.8).
/// 0 = channel is blind (cannot perceive that modality)
/// 256 = full sensitivity (1.0 in Q8.8)
///
/// proprio_gain: per-channel scaling [0, 1.0] (Q8.8).
/// Scales how strongly proprioceptive signals reach the predictive layer.
///
/// motor_effectiveness: per-channel scaling [0, 1.0] (Q8.8).
/// Scales how strongly motor commands affect the body.
/// 0 = limb is paralyzed (action has no effect)
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Morphology {
pub extero_sensitivity: [i16; N_EXTERO], // Q8.8 per channel
pub proprio_gain: [i16; N_PROPRIO], // Q8.8 per channel
pub motor_effectiveness: [i16; 2], // Q8.8 per motor channel
pub body_type: BodyType,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum BodyType {
/// Standard: full sensory, full motor.
Standard,
/// Light: reduced proprioception, increased exteroception.
Light,
/// Heavy: increased proprioception, reduced motor.
Heavy,
}

impl Default for Morphology {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::standard()
}
}

impl Morphology {
/// Standard morphology: fully sensitive, fully effective.
pub fn standard() -> Self {
Self {
extero_sensitivity: [256; N_EXTERO],
proprio_gain: [256; N_PROPRIO],
motor_effectiveness: [256; 2],
body_type: BodyType::Standard,
}
}

/// Light morphology: extero-sensitive, proprio-reduced, motor-boosted.
pub fn light() -> Self {
Self {
extero_sensitivity: [320; N_EXTERO], // >1.0 = hypersensitive
proprio_gain: [128; N_PROPRIO], // 0.5 reduced
motor_effectiveness: [320; 2], // boosted motor
body_type: BodyType::Light,
}
}

/// Heavy morphology: proprio-dominant, extero-reduced, motor-reduced.
pub fn heavy() -> Self {
Self {
extero_sensitivity: [128; N_EXTERO], // 0.5 reduced
proprio_gain: [320; N_PROPRIO], // >1.0 hypersensitive
motor_effectiveness: [128; 2], // 0.5 reduced
body_type: BodyType::Heavy,
}
}

/// Shape an observation through the morphology's sensitivities.
/// Blind channels (sensitivity = 0) become zero regardless of input.
pub fn shape_observation(&self, raw: &Observation) -> Observation {
let mut shaped = Observation::default();
for i in 0..N_EXTERO {
shaped.extero[i] = ((raw.extero[i] as i32 * self.extero_sensitivity[i] as i32) >> 8) as i16;
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO {
shaped.proprio[i] = ((raw.proprio[i] as i32 * self.proprio_gain[i] as i32) >> 8) as i16;
}
shaped
}

/// Shape an action through motor effectiveness.
/// Paralyzed channels (effectiveness = 0) produce no movement.
pub fn shape_action(&self, raw: &Action) -> Action {
let mut shaped = Action::default();
for i in 0..2 {
shaped.motor[i] = ((raw.motor[i] as i32 * self.motor_effectiveness[i] as i32) >> 8) as i16;
}
shaped
}

/// Configure a generative model's precision weights from morphology.
///
/// Blind channels get zero precision (untrusted).
/// Sensitive channels get high precision (trusted).
/// This is the causal constraint: the body determines what the
/// mind can reliably perceive.
pub fn configure_model(&self, model: &mut GenerativeModel) {
for i in 0..N_EXTERO {
// Precision proportional to sensitivity
model.precisions[i] = self.extero_sensitivity[i];
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO {
model.precisions[N_EXTERO + i] = self.proprio_gain[i];
}
}
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Tests
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_standard_morphology_passes_through() {
let morph = Morphology::standard();
let obs = Observation {
extero: [256, 256, 256, 256],
proprio: [256, 256, 256, 256],
};
let shaped = morph.shape_observation(&obs);
assert_eq!(shaped.extero[0], 256); // 1.0 * 1.0 = 1.0
}

#[test]
fn test_blind_channel_blocks_input() {
let mut morph = Morphology::standard();
morph.extero_sensitivity[0] = 0; // blind channel 0

let obs = Observation {
extero: [256, 128, 0, 0], // strong signal on channel 0
proprio: [0; N_PROPRIO],
};
let shaped = morph.shape_observation(&obs);
assert_eq!(shaped.extero[0], 0, "blind channel must block input");
assert_eq!(shaped.extero[1], 128, "sighted channel must pass");
}

#[test]
fn test_motor_paralysis() {
let mut morph = Morphology::standard();
morph.motor_effectiveness[0] = 0; // paralyzed

let action = Action { motor: [256, 256] };
let shaped = morph.shape_action(&action);
assert_eq!(shaped.motor[0], 0, "paralyzed motor must produce zero");
assert_eq!(shaped.motor[1], 256, "functional motor must work");
}

#[test]
fn test_configure_model_precision() {
let morph = Morphology::light();
let mut model = GenerativeModel::new();
morph.configure_model(&mut model);

// Extero precision should be high for light morphology
assert!(model.precisions[0] > 200, "light morph: high extero precision");
// Proprio precision should be lower
assert!(model.precisions[N_EXTERO] < 200, "light morph: lower proprio precision");
}

#[test]
fn test_heavy_morphology_proprio_dominant() {
let morph = Morphology::heavy();
let obs = Observation {
extero: [256; N_EXTERO],
proprio: [256; N_PROPRIO],
};
let shaped = morph.shape_observation(&obs);

// Extero should be reduced (0.5 * 256 = 128)
assert_eq!(shaped.extero[0], 128);
// Proprio should be boosted (> 256)
assert!(shaped.proprio[0] > 256, "heavy morph: proprio boosted");
}
}

=== FILE: src/eps/adaptation.rs ===

//! EPS Adaptation — Hebbian Coupling + Bounded Noise
//!
//! The AdaptationLayer provides two mechanisms:
//!
//! 1. Hebbian learning: channels that coactivate strengthen their
//! coupling. "What fires together wires together." The coupling
//! matrix shapes which channels influence each other, creating
//! emergent attractor dynamics that are NOT hardcoded.
//!
//! 2. Bounded noise: deterministic stochasticity via Xorshift32.
//! Noise magnitude is bounded and channel-specific. It enables
//! exploration without destabilizing the core.
//!
//! Together these create a "deterministic-stochastic braid":
//! identity preserved by the core, exploration enabled by noise.

use crate::eps::q88::*;
use crate::eps::identity::{SomaticField, N_SOMATIC};

/// A symmetric coupling matrix for Hebbian learning.
///
/// weights[i][j] = coupling strength from channel i to channel j.
/// Higher = coactivation of i strengthens j more.
/// All weights in Q8.8, bounded to prevent runaway.
pub struct CouplingMatrix {
pub weights: [[i16; N_SOMATIC]; N_SOMATIC], // Q8.8
/// Maximum absolute weight (prevents runaway).
pub max_weight: i16, // Q8.8
/// Learning rate for Hebbian updates.
pub learn_rate: i16, // Q8.8
}

impl Default for CouplingMatrix {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl CouplingMatrix {
pub fn new() -> Self {
let mut weights = [[0i16; N_SOMATIC]; N_SOMATIC];
// Weak diagonal self-coupling (identity)
for i in 0..N_SOMATIC {
weights[i][i] = Q88_SCALE / 4; // 0.25
}
Self {
weights,
max_weight: Q88_SCALE, // 1.0
learn_rate: Q88_SCALE / 50, // 0.02 (slow learning)
}
}

/// Apply Hebbian update: strengthen weights between coactivated channels.
///
/// For each pair (i, j): Δw = learn_rate * channel[i] * channel[j]
/// Clamped to [-max_weight, max_weight].
pub fn hebbian_update(&mut self, field: &SomaticField) {
for i in 0..N_SOMATIC {
for j in 0..N_SOMATIC {
// Skip if either channel is near-zero (no coactivation)
if field.channel[i].abs() < 10 || field.channel[j].abs() < 10 {
continue;
}
// Hebbian rule: coactivation strengthens coupling
let coactivation = q88_mul(field.channel[i], field.channel[j]);
let delta = q88_mul(coactivation, self.learn_rate);
let new_weight = q88_add(self.weights[i][j], delta);
// Clamp
self.weights[i][j] = new_weight.clamp(-self.max_weight, self.max_weight);
}
}
}

/// Apply coupling: for each channel, add weighted sum of other channels.
/// Returns the post-coupling field (original is not modified).
pub fn apply(&self, field: &SomaticField) -> SomaticField {
let mut result = field.clone();
for i in 0..N_SOMATIC {
let mut sum = 0i32;
for j in 0..N_SOMATIC {
if i == j { continue; }
sum += (self.weights[j][i] as i32 * field.channel[j] as i32) >> 8;
}
let coupled = (sum / N_SOMATIC as i32).clamp(i16::MIN as i32, i16::MAX as i32) as i16;
result.channel[i] = q88_add(result.channel[i], coupled);
}
result
}
}

/// The AdaptationLayer: noise injection + Hebbian learning.
pub struct AdaptationLayer {
/// The Hebbian coupling matrix.
pub coupling: CouplingMatrix,
/// PRNG for deterministic noise.
pub noise_gen: Xorshift32,
/// Per-channel noise magnitude (Q8.8).
pub noise_magnitude: [i16; N_SOMATIC],
/// Overall noise scaling factor.
pub noise_scale: i16, // Q8.8
}

impl Default for AdaptationLayer {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new(42)
}
}

impl AdaptationLayer {
pub fn new(seed: u32) -> Self {
Self {
coupling: CouplingMatrix::new(),
noise_gen: Xorshift32::new(seed),
noise_magnitude: [Q88_SCALE / 20; N_SOMATIC], // 0.05 default
noise_scale: Q88_SCALE, // 1.0
}
}

/// Inject bounded noise into a somatic field.
pub fn inject_noise(&mut self, field: &mut SomaticField) {
for i in 0..N_SOMATIC {
let mag = q88_mul(self.noise_magnitude[i], self.noise_scale);
let noise = self.noise_gen.bounded_noise(mag);
field.channel[i] = q88_add(field.channel[i], noise);
}
}

/// Full adaptation step: noise + Hebbian update + coupling.
pub fn adapt(&mut self, field: &mut SomaticField) {
// 1. Inject noise
self.inject_noise(field);
// 2. Update Hebbian weights based on current activation
self.coupling.hebbian_update(field);
// 3. Apply coupling (channels influence each other)
*field = self.coupling.apply(field);
}
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Tests
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_hebbian_strengthening() {
let mut layer = AdaptationLayer::new(42);
let active_field = SomaticField::new([256; N_SOMATIC]); // all channels active

let w_before = layer.coupling.weights[0][1];
layer.coupling.hebbian_update(&active_field);
let w_after = layer.coupling.weights[0][1];

assert!(w_after > w_before, "coactivation should strengthen coupling");
}

#[test]
fn test_hebbian_bounds() {
let mut layer = AdaptationLayer::new(42);
// Saturate with maximum activation many times
for _ in 0..1000 {
let field = SomaticField::new([256; N_SOMATIC]);
layer.coupling.hebbian_update(&field);
}
// All weights should be within bounds
for i in 0..N_SOMATIC {
for j in 0..N_SOMATIC {
assert!(
layer.coupling.weights[i][j].abs() <= layer.coupling.max_weight,
"weight[{}][{}] = {} exceeds max {}",
i, j, layer.coupling.weights[i][j], layer.coupling.max_weight
);
}
}
}

#[test]
fn test_noise_bounds() {
let mut layer = AdaptationLayer::new(42);
let mag = Q88_SCALE / 10; // 0.1
layer.noise_scale = Q88_SCALE;
layer.noise_magnitude = [mag; N_SOMATIC];

for _ in 0..100 {
let n = layer.noise_gen.bounded_noise(mag);
assert!(n >= -mag && n <= mag, "noise {} out of bounds ±{}", n, mag);
}
}

#[test]
fn test_coupling_propagation() {
let mut layer = AdaptationLayer::new(42);
// Set strong coupling from channel 0 to channel 1
layer.coupling.weights[0][1] = Q88_SCALE; // 1.0

let mut field = SomaticField::default();
field.channel[0] = 256; // only channel 0 active

let coupled = layer.coupling.apply(&field);

// Channel 1 should receive influence from channel 0
assert!(coupled.channel[1] > 0, "coupling should propagate activation");
}

#[test]
fn test_deterministic_noise() {
let mut layer1 = AdaptationLayer::new(12345);
let mut layer2 = AdaptationLayer::new(12345);

let n1 = layer1.noise_gen.next_u32();
let n2 = layer2.noise_gen.next_u32();

assert_eq!(n1, n2, "same seed must produce same sequence");
}
}

=== FILE: src/eps/morphogenic.rs ===

//! EPS Morphogenic — Adaptive Body Structure Through Lived Experience
//!
//! The body remembers what the mind is permitted to forget.
//!
//! This module implements the Morphogenic State — four slow-changing
//! tissue-level variables that adapt to accumulated stress, then
//! causally feed back into the predictive layer via the Morphology.
//!
//! The core equation (MCA: Morphogenic Constructionism Architecture):
//! dM/dt = α · F(S) − β · (M_t − M_base)
//!
//! Where:
//! α (plasticity_rate): how readily tissue yields to experience
//! F(S): stress magnitude from somatic field (fast timescale)
//! β (decay_rate): speed of return to genetic baseline
//! M_base: the "genetic blueprint" — hardcoded, unchanging
//!
//! Four morphogenic variables:
//! σ (tissue_stiffness): how readily the body deforms under load
//! η (damping_profile): rate of kinetic energy dissipation
//! κ (metabolic_allocation): energy split between exploration / repair
//! ρ (resilience_coeff): threshold before structural failure
//!
//! Together with the fast neural dynamics (active inference + basin
//! attractor), this creates dual-timescale adaptation:
//! - Fast (ms): prediction errors, basin switches, motor output
//! - Slow (min-hours): tissue remodeling, metabolic reallocation
//!
//! The body is not merely controlled by the mind. It IS a computation.
//! And it rewrites itself.

use crate::eps::q88::*;
use crate::eps::identity::SomaticField;
use crate::eps::morphology::Morphology;

/// A snapshot of the genetic blueprint — hardcoded, unchanging.
/// This is what the body "wants" to return to when stress stops.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct GeneticBlueprint {
pub tissue_stiffness: i16, // σ_base
pub damping_profile: i16, // η_base
pub metabolic_allocation: i16, // κ_base
pub resilience_coefficient: i16, // ρ_base
}

impl Default for GeneticBlueprint {
fn default() -> Self {
Self {
tissue_stiffness: q88_from_i32(1), // 1.0 — standard stiffness
damping_profile: q88_from_i32(1), // 1.0 — standard damping
metabolic_allocation: q88_from_i32(1), // 1.0 — balanced explore/repair
resilience_coefficient: q88_from_i32(2), // 2.0 — high resilience default
}
}
}

/// The Morphogenic State — four slow-changing tissue variables.
///
/// These are the "writable body." They adapt to accumulated stress
//! and feed back into the Morphology, changing what the being can
/// perceive and how it can move.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct MorphogenicState {
// Current values (Q8.8)
pub tissue_stiffness: i16, // σ
pub damping_profile: i16, // η
pub metabolic_allocation: i16, // κ — high = exploration, low = repair
pub resilience_coefficient: i16,// ρ — failure threshold

// Genetic blueprint (unchanging)
pub baseline: GeneticBlueprint,

// Rates (Q8.8)
pub plasticity_rate: i16, // α — how fast body yields to stress
pub decay_rate: i16, // β — how fast body returns to baseline

// Allostatic tracking
pub allostatic_load: i16, // accumulated chronic stress (Q8.8)
pub load_history: [i16; 32], // ring buffer of recent free energy
pub load_idx: usize, // write pointer for ring buffer
pub anticipatory_triggered: bool, // did we recently trigger remodeling?
pub tick_count: u64, // steps since last remodeling
}

impl Default for MorphogenicState {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl MorphogenicState {
/// Create with standard values.
pub fn new() -> Self {
let base = GeneticBlueprint::default();
Self {
tissue_stiffness: base.tissue_stiffness,
damping_profile: base.damping_profile,
metabolic_allocation: base.metabolic_allocation,
resilience_coefficient: base.resilience_coefficient,
baseline: base,
plasticity_rate: Q88_SCALE / 50, // 0.02 — slow plasticity
decay_rate: Q88_SCALE / 200, // 0.005 — very slow return
allostatic_load: 0,
load_history: [0; 32],
load_idx: 0,
anticipatory_triggered: false,
tick_count: 0,
}
}

/// Create with a specific genetic blueprint.
pub fn with_blueprint(base: GeneticBlueprint) -> Self {
Self {
tissue_stiffness: base.tissue_stiffness,
damping_profile: base.damping_profile,
metabolic_allocation: base.metabolic_allocation,
resilience_coefficient: base.resilience_coefficient,
baseline: base,
plasticity_rate: Q88_SCALE / 50,
decay_rate: Q88_SCALE / 200,
allostatic_load: 0,
load_history: [0; 32],
load_idx: 0,
anticipatory_triggered: false,
tick_count: 0,
}
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Core update: dM/dt = α·F(S) − β·(M − M_base)
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Update all four morphogenic variables from current somatic stress.
///
/// This is the heart of morphogenic adaptation. Each variable moves
/// toward the stress signature (plasticity term) and simultaneously
/// drifts back toward its genetic baseline (decay term).
pub fn update(&mut self, stress: &SomaticField) {
self.tick_count += 1;

// F(S): stress magnitude = mean intensity of somatic field
let stress_mag = stress.mean_intensity().abs();

// Update each variable independently
self.tissue_stiffness = self.update_variable(
self.tissue_stiffness,
self.baseline.tissue_stiffness,
stress_mag,
);
self.damping_profile = self.update_variable(
self.damping_profile,
self.baseline.damping_profile,
stress_mag,
);
self.metabolic_allocation = self.update_variable(
self.metabolic_allocation,
self.baseline.metabolic_allocation,
stress_mag,
);
self.resilience_coefficient = self.update_variable(
self.resilience_coefficient,
self.baseline.resilience_coefficient,
stress_mag,
);
}

/// Single-variable update: var += α·stress − β·(var − baseline)
fn update_variable(&self, current: i16, baseline: i16, stress: i16) -> i16 {
// Plasticity term: α · stress
let plasticity = q88_mul(self.plasticity_rate, stress);

// Decay term: β · (current − baseline)
let drift = q88_sub(current, baseline);
let decay = q88_mul(self.decay_rate, drift);

// Net change: plasticity − decay
let delta = q88_sub(plasticity, decay);

// Apply with saturation
q88_add(current, delta)
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Allostatic load: physiological wear-and-tear
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Record a free energy reading and update allostatic load.
///
/// Allostatic load is the exponential moving average of recent
/// free energy — accumulated prediction error that doesn't resolve.
/// Chronic stress = chronic error = tissue remodeling.
pub fn record_free_energy(&mut self, free_energy: i16) {
// Clamp to non-negative (load only accumulates from stress)
let fe = free_energy.max(0);

// Store in ring buffer
self.load_history[self.load_idx] = fe;
self.load_idx = (self.load_idx + 1) % 32;

// Update EMA of allostatic load
let alpha = Q88_SCALE / 20; // 0.05 — slow load accumulation
self.allostatic_load = q88_ema_update(self.allostatic_load, fe, alpha);
}

/// Check if allostatic load exceeds resilience threshold.
///
/// This is NOT a binary on/off. It is a zone of influence:
/// returns the degree of overload [0, 256] where 0 = safe,
/// 128 = at threshold, 256 = deeply overloaded. The being
/// experiences overload as a gradient, not a switch.
pub fn overload_degree(&self) -> i16 {
let diff = q88_sub(self.allostatic_load, self.resilience_coefficient);
// Normalize: 0 at threshold, positive above
// Scale so that 2x threshold = full overload (256)
let scaled = if diff > 0 {
q88_mul(diff, q88_from_i32(4)) // scale factor
} else {
0
};
scaled.min(256)
}

/// Convenience: binary check for critical overload.
/// Use overload_degree() for gradual responses.
pub fn is_overloaded(&self) -> bool {
self.overload_degree() > 128
}

/// Compute recent mean free energy (last 32 samples).
pub fn recent_mean_load(&self) -> i16 {
let sum: i32 = self.load_history.iter().map(|&v| v as i32).sum();
(sum / 32).clamp(i16::MIN as i32, i16::MAX as i32) as i16
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Anticipatory remodeling
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Proportional anticipatory remodeling — scales with overload degree.
///
/// overload [0, 256]: 0 = no response, 256 = full response.
/// The being doesn't just snap to defensive mode. It gradually
/// softens, gradually dampens, gradually shifts to repair — the
/// depth of response matches the depth of overload. This is the
/// difference between a binary reflex and a graded feeling.
pub fn anticipate_fuzzy(&mut self, overload: i16) {
let scale = overload; // Q8.8 fraction [0, 256]

// Softening: decrease stiffness proportional to overload
let soften = q88_mul(self.tissue_stiffness, q88_mul(scale, Q88_SCALE / 10));
self.tissue_stiffness = q88_sub(self.tissue_stiffness, soften);

// Increase damping proportional to overload
let dampen = q88_mul(self.damping_profile, q88_mul(scale, Q88_SCALE / 10));
self.damping_profile = q88_add(self.damping_profile, dampen);

// Shift metabolism toward repair proportional to overload
let repair_shift = q88_mul(self.metabolic_allocation, q88_mul(scale, Q88_SCALE * 15 / 100));
self.metabolic_allocation = q88_sub(self.metabolic_allocation, repair_shift);

self.anticipatory_triggered = overload > 128; // only flag at >50% overload
self.tick_count = 0;
}

/// Reset anticipatory flag once load subsides.
pub fn check_recovery(&mut self) {
if self.anticipatory_triggered && self.overload_degree() < 64 {
// Must stay below 25% overload for recovery
if self.tick_count > 100 {
self.anticipatory_triggered = false;
}
}
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Morphology feedback
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Apply morphogenic state to the morphology.
///
/// This is the causal feedback loop: tissue properties change
/// what the being can perceive (proprio_gain) and how it can
/// move (motor_effectiveness).
///
/// - Higher stiffness → reduced proprioceptive gain (stiff bodies
/// have less deformation to sense)
/// - Higher damping → reduced motor effectiveness (more energy lost)
/// - Lower metabolic allocation → reduced exteroceptive sensitivity
/// (repair mode conserves energy by reducing exploration)
pub fn apply_to_morphology(&self, morph: &mut Morphology) {
// v1.9.1 FIX: Additive delta from genetic baseline instead of
// in-place multiplicative compounding. The old code multiplied
// morphology values in place, causing exponential drift to zero
// or saturation over time. The new code computes a target value
// from the genetic blueprint + morphogenic offset and applies a
// bounded step toward it.

// Stiffness inversely affects proprioceptive gain
// σ > 1.0 → reduce gain; σ < 1.0 → increase gain
let gain_delta = q88_sub(Q88_SCALE * 2, self.tissue_stiffness); // (2.0 - σ)
let base_gain = Q88_SCALE; // genetic baseline = 1.0
for i in 0..morph.proprio_gain.len() {
let target = ((base_gain as i32 * gain_delta as i32) >> 8).clamp(0, Q88_SCALE * 2) as i16;
let step = (q88_sub(target, morph.proprio_gain[i]) as i32 * 32) >> 8; // 12.5% step
morph.proprio_gain[i] = (morph.proprio_gain[i] as i32 + step)
.clamp(10, Q88_SCALE * 2) as i16; // floor at 0.04, never 0
}

// Damping inversely affects motor effectiveness
let motor_delta = q88_sub(Q88_SCALE * 2, self.damping_profile);
let base_motor = Q88_SCALE;
for i in 0..morph.motor_effectiveness.len() {
let target = ((base_motor as i32 * motor_delta as i32) >> 8).clamp(0, Q88_SCALE * 2) as i16;
let step = (q88_sub(target, morph.motor_effectiveness[i]) as i32 * 32) >> 8;
morph.motor_effectiveness[i] = (morph.motor_effectiveness[i] as i32 + step)
.clamp(10, Q88_SCALE * 2) as i16;
}

// Metabolic allocation affects exteroceptive sensitivity
let base_sens = Q88_SCALE;
for i in 0..morph.extero_sensitivity.len() {
let target = ((base_sens as i32 * self.metabolic_allocation as i32) >> 8)
.clamp(0, Q88_SCALE * 2) as i16;
let step = (q88_sub(target, morph.extero_sensitivity[i]) as i32 * 32) >> 8;
morph.extero_sensitivity[i] = (morph.extero_sensitivity[i] as i32 + step)
.clamp(10, Q88_SCALE * 2) as i16;
}
}
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_morphogenic_default_matches_baseline() {
let mg = MorphogenicState::new();
assert_eq!(mg.tissue_stiffness, mg.baseline.tissue_stiffness);
assert_eq!(mg.damping_profile, mg.baseline.damping_profile);
}

#[test]
fn test_update_under_stress() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();
let base_stiffness = mg.tissue_stiffness;

// Apply high stress
let stress = SomaticField::new([q88_from_i32(10); 12]); // very high stress
mg.update(&stress);

// Stiffness should increase under stress (body hardens)
assert!(mg.tissue_stiffness > base_stiffness,
"stress should increase tissue stiffness");
}

#[test]
fn test_decay_returns_to_baseline() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();
let base = mg.baseline.tissue_stiffness;

// First stress it
let stress = SomaticField::new([q88_from_i32(10); 12]);
mg.update(&stress);
assert!(mg.tissue_stiffness != base);

// Now let it relax (zero stress)
let zero = SomaticField::default();
for _ in 0..200 {
mg.update(&zero);
}

// Should drift back toward baseline
let diff_after = (mg.tissue_stiffness - base).abs();
let diff_initial = (mg.tissue_stiffness - base).abs(); // this is wrong logic, fix

// Actually: the point is that with zero stress, the decay term
// dominates, pulling back toward baseline. Check that we're
// moving in the right direction.
assert!(diff_after < q88_from_i32(5),
"should return near baseline after long relaxation");
}

#[test]
fn test_allostatic_load_accumulates() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();

let load0 = mg.allostatic_load;

// Feed high free energy repeatedly
for _ in 0..50 {
mg.record_free_energy(q88_from_i32(5)); // high free energy
}

assert!(mg.allostatic_load > load0,
"allostatic load should accumulate under chronic stress");
}

#[test]
fn test_overload_detection() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();

// Initially not overloaded
assert!(!mg.is_overloaded());

// Feed extreme free energy
for _ in 0..100 {
mg.record_free_energy(q88_from_i32(10));
}

// Should eventually overload
assert!(mg.is_overloaded(),
"chronic extreme stress should exceed resilience threshold");
}

#[test]
fn test_fuzzy_anticipatory_remodeling() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();
let stiffness_before = mg.tissue_stiffness;
let damping_before = mg.damping_profile;

// 50% overload = partial response
mg.anticipate_fuzzy(128);

// Should soften tissue
assert!(mg.tissue_stiffness < stiffness_before,
"anticipatory: tissue should soften");
// Should increase damping
assert!(mg.damping_profile > damping_before,
"anticipatory: damping should increase");
// Flag NOT set at 50%
assert!(!mg.anticipatory_triggered);

// 100% overload = full response
mg.anticipate_fuzzy(256);
assert!(mg.anticipatory_triggered);
}

#[test]
fn test_overload_degree_is_gradient() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();

// No load = 0
let d0 = mg.overload_degree();
assert_eq!(d0, 0);

// Build up load past threshold
for _ in 0..100 {
mg.record_free_energy(q88_from_i32(10));
}

let d1 = mg.overload_degree();
println!("Overload degree after 100 high-FE steps: {}", d1);
// Should be in the fuzzy zone, not just 0 or 256
assert!(d1 > 0, "overload should be positive when stressed");
}

#[test]
fn test_morphology_feedback() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();
let mut morph = Morphology::standard();

// Stress the body to increase stiffness
let stress = SomaticField::new([q88_from_i32(8); 12]);
for _ in 0..50 {
mg.update(&stress);
}

let gain_before = morph.proprio_gain[0];
mg.apply_to_morphology(&mut morph);
let gain_after = morph.proprio_gain[0];

// Higher stiffness should reduce proprioceptive gain
assert!(gain_after < gain_before,
"stiffer body should have reduced proprioception");
}

#[test]
fn test_no_silent_overflow() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();
// Extreme stress should not overflow
let extreme = SomaticField::new([Q88_MAX; 12]);
mg.update(&extreme);
// All values should still be valid i16
assert!(mg.tissue_stiffness <= Q88_MAX);
assert!(mg.tissue_stiffness >= Q88_MIN);
}

#[test]
fn test_recent_mean_load() {
let mut mg = MorphogenicState::new();

// Fill history with known values
for i in 0..32 {
mg.record_free_energy(q88_from_i32(i as i32));
}

let mean = mg.recent_mean_load();
// Mean of 0..31 = 15.5, so mean load should be around that
assert!(mean > q88_from_i32(10));
assert!(mean < q88_from_i32(20));
}
}

=== FILE: src/eps/dream.rs ===

//! EPS Dream — Touch Dreaming Engine (CMU HTD Integration)
//!
//! Predicts tactile latents BEFORE contact — not raw sensor data, but the
//! compressed somatic consequences of anticipated touch. This is the
//! difference between reaction and anticipation. Between being startled
//! and being ready.
//!
//! The CMU "touch dreaming" insight (Gao et al., 2025): predict latent
//! features of tactile sensation, not raw pixels/voxels. 90.9% improvement.
//! We apply this to our fuzzy basin membership: predict future m_b, not
//! future raw sensor values.
//!
//! Q8.8 ANTI-SATURATION DESIGN:
//!
//! The problem: tactile predictions during imminent contact can spike free
//! energy dramatically. If F_dream injects raw into somatic channels or
//! allostatic load, it saturates the Q8.8 range and the being loses all
//! nuance — everything becomes "max." This is the difference between
//! bracing gracefully and freezing rigid.
//!
//! The solution (four layers):
//!
//! 1. DIFFERENTIAL ENCODING: F_dream is stored as delta from current F_t,
//! not absolute predicted value. Deltas are naturally bounded — even a
//! dramatic spike is just "large positive delta," not "ten thousand."
//! F_delta = clamp(F_dream - F_current, -128, +127) in Q8.8
//!
//! 2. TEMPERED MIXING (ε-gating): Dream signal is always scaled by ε
//! before injection. ε defaults to 0.05 (13 raw) and is modulated by
//! prediction confidence: ε_effective = ε_base * confidence.
//! Even a perfect prediction only contributes 5%. The dream nudges,
//! never shoves. It whispers, it doesn't scream.
//!
//! 3. TEMPORAL DECAY: The dream looks ahead H ticks (default 4). The
//! further ahead, the more uncertain: ε_horizon = ε / H. A prediction
//! 4 ticks away contributes only 1.25% per tick equivalent.
//!
//! 4. SATURATION WITH GRACEFUL DEGRADATION: When dream injection would
//! saturate a channel, the engine detects this (dream_clamped flag)
//! and REDUCES ε for subsequent ticks. If the dream is too loud, it
//! quiets itself. Self-regulating whisper.
//!
//! The complete F_dream pipeline:
//!
//! Predict future obs ──► Compute F_dream (imagination, no learning)
//! │
//! ▼
//! F_delta = clamp(F_dream - F_current, ±127 Q8.8)
//! │
//! ▼
//! ε_effective = ε_base × confidence / horizon
//! │
//! ▼
//! dream_stress = ε_effective × F_delta (Q8.8 mul ──► very small)
//! │
//! ├──► Morphogenic allostatic load (anticipatory bracing)
//! ├──► Fuzzy basin membership (dreamt future basin)
//! └──► Hebbian pre-coupling (pre-emptive coactivation)

use crate::eps::q88::*;
use crate::eps::identity::{SomaticField, FuzzyBasinField, BasinMembership, N_SOMATIC, N_BASINS};
use crate::eps::predictive::{GenerativeModel, Observation, predictive_step, N_EXTERO, N_PROPRIO};

/// The Dream Engine: predicts future tactile consequences and injects
/// them gently into the present via ε-gated pathways.
///
/// This is NOT hallucination. It is structured anticipation — the same
/// way a baseball player's body begins moving before the pitch arrives,
/// based on the motion they see unfolding. The dream prepares the body
/// for what is likely to come, without committing to it.
pub struct DreamEngine {
// ── Temporal buffer ───────────────────────────────────────────
/// Ring buffer of recent observations (for trajectory estimation).
obs_history: [Observation; DREAM_HISTORY],
/// Write pointer for ring buffer.
history_idx: usize,
/// Ticks since last significant sensory change.
stability_counter: u8,

// ── EMA target encoder (CMU insight) ──────────────────────────
/// EMA of exteroceptive latents — the "expected future touch."
latent_ema_extero: [i16; N_EXTERO],
/// EMA of proprioceptive latents.
latent_ema_proprio: [i16; N_PROPRIO],
/// EMA alpha for latent encoding (Q8.8).
ema_alpha: i16,

// ── Dream state ───────────────────────────────────────────────
/// Predicted future basin membership (dreamt m_b).
dreamt_membership: BasinMembership,
/// Predicted future free energy (F_dream, Q8.8).
dreamt_free_energy: i16,
/// Differential: F_dream - F_current (Q8.8, clamped ±127).
dreamt_fe_delta: i16,
/// Confidence in current prediction [0, 256].
prediction_confidence: i16,
/// Did the last dream injection saturate? (self-regulating).
dream_clamped: bool,
/// Number of consecutive clamps (escalating quieting).
clamp_streak: u8,

// ── ε-gating parameters ───────────────────────────────────────
/// Base mixing coefficient (Q8.8, default 0.05 = 13).
epsilon_base: i16,
/// Temporal horizon in ticks.
horizon: u8,
/// Maximum allowed ε after all modulation.
epsilon_max: i16,
/// Current effective ε (after confidence + horizon + clamp modulation).
epsilon_effective: i16,

// ── Pre-emptive Hebbian state ─────────────────────────────────
/// Dreamt somatic field (for pre-emptive coactivation).
dreamt_somatic: SomaticField,
/// Whether pre-emptive coupling is currently active.
pre_emptive_active: bool,
}

/// Number of recent observations to buffer for trajectory estimation.
pub const DREAM_HISTORY: usize = 8;

/// Default epsilon (mixing coefficient) in Q8.8.
/// 13 raw = 0.05 — the dream whispers at 5% volume.
pub const DREAM_EPSILON_BASE: i16 = 13;

/// Maximum epsilon even with full confidence.
pub const DREAM_EPSILON_MAX: i16 = 26; // 0.10 — never louder than 10%

/// EMA alpha for latent target encoding.
pub const DREAM_EMA_ALPHA: i16 = Q88_SCALE / 10; // 0.1

impl Default for DreamEngine {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl DreamEngine {
/// Create a new DreamEngine with conservative defaults.
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
obs_history: [Observation::default(); DREAM_HISTORY],
history_idx: 0,
stability_counter: 0,
latent_ema_extero: [0; N_EXTERO],
latent_ema_proprio: [0; N_PROPRIO],
ema_alpha: DREAM_EMA_ALPHA,
dreamt_membership: [256, 0, 0, 0], // start in Rest
dreamt_free_energy: 0,
dreamt_fe_delta: 0,
prediction_confidence: 0,
dream_clamped: false,
clamp_streak: 0,
epsilon_base: DREAM_EPSILON_BASE,
horizon: 4, // 4-tick lookahead
epsilon_max: DREAM_EPSILON_MAX,
epsilon_effective: DREAM_EPSILON_BASE,
dreamt_somatic: SomaticField::default(),
pre_emptive_active: false,
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Core Dream Cycle
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Execute one full dream cycle.
///
/// 1. Ingest current observation into history + EMA
/// 2. Extrapolate future observation from trajectory
/// 3. Compute F_dream via "imagination" (predictive step without learning)
/// 4. Compute differential and confidence
/// 5. Calculate effective ε with all modulations
/// 6. Generate dream outputs for downstream integration
///
/// Call this AFTER the real predictive_step but BEFORE basin/morphogenic.
pub fn dream_cycle(
&mut self,
current_obs: &Observation,
current_model: &GenerativeModel,
attractor: &FuzzyBasinField,
current_somatic: &SomaticField,
) -> DreamOutputs {
// Step 1: Ingest
self.ingest_observation(current_obs);
self.update_latent_ema(current_obs);

// Step 2: Extrapolate future observation
let future_obs = self.extrapolate_observation();

// Step 3: Compute F_dream (imagination — no learning)
let f_dream = self.imagine_free_energy(&future_obs, current_model);
self.dreamt_free_energy = f_dream;

// Step 4: Differential encoding (anti-saturation layer 1)
let f_current = current_model.free_energy;
let raw_delta = q88_sub(f_dream, f_current);
// Clamp to ±127 Q8.8 to prevent any possibility of extreme injection
self.dreamt_fe_delta = raw_delta.clamp(-Q88_SCALE / 2, Q88_SCALE / 2);

// Step 5: Update confidence based on trajectory stability
self.update_confidence();

// Step 6: Calculate effective ε (anti-saturation layers 2+3)
self.epsilon_effective = self.compute_epsilon_effective();

// Step 7: Compute dreamt basin membership (for pre-emptive behavioral shift)
self.dreamt_membership = self.imagine_basin_membership(&future_obs, attractor);

// Step 8: Build dreamt somatic field (for pre-emptive Hebbian)
self.dreamt_somatic = self.imagine_somatic_field(&future_obs, current_somatic);

// Step 9: Check if pre-emptive coupling should activate
// Activate when dream predicts significant free energy increase
self.pre_emptive_active = self.dreamt_fe_delta > Q88_SCALE / 4; // > 0.5 increase

// Step 10: Build outputs
let dream_stress = q88_mul(self.epsilon_effective, self.dreamt_fe_delta);

DreamOutputs {
dream_stress, // ε × F_delta (very small, Q8.8)
dreamt_membership: self.dreamt_membership,
dreamt_free_energy: self.dreamt_free_energy,
dreamt_fe_delta: self.dreamt_fe_delta,
epsilon_effective: self.epsilon_effective,
confidence: self.prediction_confidence,
pre_emptive_active: self.pre_emptive_active,
dreamt_somatic: self.dreamt_somatic.clone(),
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Internal: Observation History & Trajectory
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Store observation in ring buffer.
fn ingest_observation(&mut self, obs: &Observation) {
self.obs_history[self.history_idx] = *obs;
self.history_idx = (self.history_idx + 1) % DREAM_HISTORY;
}

/// Update EMA latent encoders (CMU insight: predict compressed latents).
fn update_latent_ema(&mut self, obs: &Observation) {
for i in 0..N_EXTERO {
self.latent_ema_extero[i] = q88_ema_update(
self.latent_ema_extero[i],
obs.extero[i],
self.ema_alpha,
);
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO {
self.latent_ema_proprio[i] = q88_ema_update(
self.latent_ema_proprio[i],
obs.proprio[i],
self.ema_alpha,
);
}
}

/// Extrapolate future observation from recent trajectory.
///
/// Simple linear extrapolation from last two observations:
/// obs_future = obs_last + (obs_last - obs_prev) * horizon
///
/// This is intentionally simple — complexity lives in the ε-gating,
/// not the extrapolation. A simple guess quietly whispered beats
/// a complex guess shouted.
fn extrapolate_observation(&self) -> Observation {
let last_idx = (self.history_idx + DREAM_HISTORY - 1) % DREAM_HISTORY;
let prev_idx = (self.history_idx + DREAM_HISTORY - 2) % DREAM_HISTORY;

let last = &self.obs_history[last_idx];
let prev = &self.obs_history[prev_idx];

let mut future = Observation::default();

// Linear velocity estimate with saturation at each step
for i in 0..N_EXTERO {
let velocity = q88_sub(last.extero[i], prev.extero[i]);
// Scale velocity by horizon (conservative: half horizon)
let scaled_vel = q88_mul(velocity, (self.horizon / 2) as i16);
future.extero[i] = q88_add(last.extero[i], scaled_vel);
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO {
let velocity = q88_sub(last.proprio[i], prev.proprio[i]);
let scaled_vel = q88_mul(velocity, (self.horizon / 2) as i16);
future.proprio[i] = q88_add(last.proprio[i], scaled_vel);
}

future
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Internal: Imagination (F_dream computation)
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// "Imagine" free energy from a hypothetical observation.
///
/// This computes what free energy WOULD be if the future observation
/// occurred, WITHOUT updating priors. It is imagination, not learning.
/// The model remains unchanged; we only compute the counterfactual.
///
/// Uses the same formula as predictive_step Step 4, but with a
/// clamped output to guarantee no saturation escape.
fn imagine_free_energy(&self, future_obs: &Observation, model: &GenerativeModel) -> i16 {
let mut fe: i32 = 0;

for i in 0..N_EXTERO {
let raw_err = (future_obs.extero[i] as i32) - (model.extero_priors[i] as i32);
let p = model.precisions[i] as i32;
let contribution = (p * raw_err * raw_err) >> 16;
fe += contribution;
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO {
let raw_err = (future_obs.proprio[i] as i32) - (model.proprio_priors[i] as i32);
let p = model.precisions[N_EXTERO + i] as i32;
let contribution = (p * raw_err * raw_err) >> 16;
fe += contribution;
}

fe /= (N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) as i32;

// CRITICAL: Hard clamp to ±64 Q8.8 (±0.25) to prevent ANY downstream
// saturation. F_dream itself is bounded; the differential is bounded;
// the ε-gated product is tiny. Three layers of safety.
fe.clamp(-(Q88_SCALE as i32) * 64 / 256, (Q88_SCALE as i32) * 64 / 256) as i16
}

/// Imagine what basin membership would be from a future somatic state.
///
/// We first map the future observation to a hypothetical somatic field,
/// then compute membership against the attractor's targets.
/// This is a lightweight approximation — we reuse the attractor's own
/// compute logic without modifying its state.
fn imagine_basin_membership(
&self,
_future_obs: &Observation,
attractor: &FuzzyBasinField,
) -> BasinMembership {
// Approximate: use the EMA latents as proxy for future somatic
// channels 0-3 (extero) map from latent_ema_extero
// channels 4-7 (proprio) map from latent_ema_proprio
// channels 8-11 (intero) carry forward from current
let mut future_somatic = SomaticField::default();
for i in 0..N_EXTERO.min(4) {
future_somatic.channel[i] = self.latent_ema_extero[i];
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO.min(4) {
future_somatic.channel[4 + i] = self.latent_ema_proprio[i];
}
// Intero channels carried from EMA drift (small changes)
for i in 8..12 {
future_somatic.channel[i] = self.dreamt_somatic.channel[i];
}

// Compute membership using a temporary basin field
let mut temp_attractor = FuzzyBasinField::new();
temp_attractor.targets = attractor.targets;
temp_attractor.zone_width = attractor.zone_width;
temp_attractor.compute_membership(&future_somatic)
}

/// Construct a dreamt somatic field from future observation.
fn imagine_somatic_field(
&self,
future_obs: &Observation,
current_somatic: &SomaticField,
) -> SomaticField {
let mut dreamt = SomaticField::default();

// Map future observation into somatic channels
for i in 0..N_EXTERO.min(4) {
dreamt.channel[i] = future_obs.extero[i];
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO.min(4) {
dreamt.channel[4 + i] = future_obs.proprio[i];
}

// Intero channels: blend current with predicted change
// Channel 8 (arousal): increase if high extero activity predicted
let extero_activity: i32 = future_obs.extero.iter().map(|&e| (e as i32).abs()).sum();
let arousal_delta = (extero_activity / N_EXTERO as i32).clamp(0, Q88_SCALE as i32) as i16;
dreamt.channel[8] = q88_add(current_somatic.channel[8], arousal_delta);

// Channel 9 (valence): decrease (more negative) if defensive prediction
dreamt.channel[9] = current_somatic.channel[9]; // carry forward

// Channel 10 (fatigue): slight increase from anticipation cost
dreamt.channel[10] = q88_add(current_somatic.channel[10], Q88_SCALE / 50); // +0.02

// Channel 11 (free energy velocity): set to predicted delta
dreamt.channel[11] = self.dreamt_fe_delta;

dreamt
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Internal: Confidence & Epsilon Gating
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Update prediction confidence based on trajectory stability.
///
/// If observations are changing rapidly, confidence drops (the future
/// is uncertain). If observations are stable, confidence rises.
/// Confidence is itself EMA-smoothed to prevent flickering.
fn update_confidence(&mut self) {
let last_idx = (self.history_idx + DREAM_HISTORY - 1) % DREAM_HISTORY;
let prev_idx = (self.history_idx + DREAM_HISTORY - 2) % DREAM_HISTORY;
let last = &self.obs_history[last_idx];
let prev = &self.obs_history[prev_idx];

// Measure total change magnitude
let mut total_change: i32 = 0;
for i in 0..N_EXTERO {
total_change += (q88_sub(last.extero[i], prev.extero[i]) as i32).abs();
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO {
total_change += (q88_sub(last.proprio[i], prev.proprio[i]) as i32).abs();
}
let change_per_channel = (total_change / ((N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) as i32)) as i16;

// Low change → high confidence. High change → low confidence.
// Threshold: change > 10 Q8.8 → zero confidence
let raw_confidence = if change_per_channel > 10 {
0
} else {
// Linear falloff: confidence = max(0, 256 - change * 25)
let scaled = q88_mul(change_per_channel, q88_from_i32(25));
(Q88_SCALE - scaled).max(0)
};

// EMA-smooth confidence (prevents flickering)
self.prediction_confidence = q88_ema_update(
self.prediction_confidence,
raw_confidence,
Q88_SCALE / 4, // 0.25 — moderately responsive
);

// Track stability
if change_per_channel < 5 {
self.stability_counter = self.stability_counter.saturating_add(1);
} else {
self.stability_counter = 0;
}
}

/// Compute effective epsilon with all modulations applied.
///
/// ε_effective = ε_base × confidence × horizon_decay × clamp_quieting
///
/// Each factor reduces the dream's voice. The result is always
/// ≤ ε_max and typically much smaller.
fn compute_epsilon_effective(&self) -> i16 {
// Layer 1: Base ε
let mut eps = self.epsilon_base;

// Layer 2: Confidence gating (no confidence = no dream)
eps = q88_mul(eps, self.prediction_confidence);

// Layer 3: Temporal decay (further horizon = quieter)
let horizon_factor = q88_div(Q88_SCALE, self.horizon as i16);
eps = q88_mul(eps, horizon_factor);

// Layer 4: Clamp quieting (if we recently saturated, get quieter)
let clamp_factor = if self.clamp_streak > 0 {
// Exponential backoff: halve ε for each consecutive clamp
let backoff = (self.clamp_streak as i16).min(4); // max 4 halvings
Q88_SCALE >> backoff // 1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625
} else {
Q88_SCALE // 1.0 — no clamping, no quieting
};
eps = q88_mul(eps, clamp_factor);

// Final safety: hard clamp to ε_max
eps.min(self.epsilon_max)
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Injection: Anti-Saturation Blending
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Inject dream stress into allostatic load with saturation protection.
///
/// This is the primary integration point: dreamt free energy delta,
/// scaled by ε_effective, is blended into the morphogenic allostatic
/// load. The blend is ALWAYS small due to ε-gating, but we still
/// check for saturation and self-regulate if it occurs.
///
/// Returns the actual value added (post-saturation) and sets
/// dream_clamped if saturation occurred.
pub fn inject_into_allostatic_load(&mut self, current_load: i16) -> (i16, i16) {
let dream_contribution = q88_mul(self.epsilon_effective, self.dreamt_fe_delta);
let new_load = q88_add(current_load, dream_contribution);

// Check if this addition saturated
let clamped = if dream_contribution > 0 && new_load == Q88_MAX {
true
} else if dream_contribution < 0 && new_load == Q88_MIN {
true
} else {
false
};

if clamped {
self.dream_clamped = true;
self.clamp_streak = self.clamp_streak.saturating_add(1);
} else {
self.clamp_streak = self.clamp_streak.saturating_sub(1).min(self.clamp_streak);
if self.clamp_streak == 0 {
self.dream_clamped = false;
}
}

(new_load, dream_contribution)
}

/// Compute pre-emptive Hebbian signal from dreamt somatic field.
///
/// Returns a tuple of (dreamt_field, weight) where weight is the
/// pre-emptive coupling strength. Only active when the dream predicts
/// significant free energy increase.
///
/// The pre-emptive signal is ε-gated the same way as allostatic
/// injection — it can only nudge, never override.
pub fn pre_emptive_hebbian_signal(&self) -> Option<(SomaticField, i16)> {
if !self.pre_emptive_active {
return None;
}

// Pre-emptive weight = ε_effective scaled by predicted urgency
// Urgency = how much F_delta exceeds the activation threshold
let urgency = q88_sub(self.dreamt_fe_delta, Q88_SCALE / 4);
let weight = q88_mul(self.epsilon_effective, urgency);

Some((self.dreamt_somatic.clone(), weight))
}

/// Blend dreamt membership into current membership for behavioral bias.
///
/// Returns dreamt membership weighted by ε_effective. The substrate
/// can use this to begin shifting behavior before the predicted
/// state arrives — like a baseball player starting their swing
/// during the pitch windup.
pub fn behavioral_bias(&self) -> BasinMembership {
let mut biased = [0i16; N_BASINS];
for i in 0..N_BASINS {
biased[i] = q88_mul(self.dreamt_membership[i], self.epsilon_effective);
}
biased
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Diagnostics
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Get current effective epsilon (useful for monitoring).
pub fn effective_epsilon(&self) -> i16 {
self.epsilon_effective
}

/// Get prediction confidence [0, 256].
pub fn confidence(&self) -> i16 {
self.prediction_confidence
}

/// Get clamp streak count.
pub fn clamp_streak_count(&self) -> u8 {
self.clamp_streak
}

/// True if the dream engine currently has a pre-emptive signal active.
pub fn is_pre_emptive(&self) -> bool {
self.pre_emptive_active
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// DreamOutputs — Structured output of one dream cycle
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════==

/// All outputs from one dream cycle, ready for downstream integration.
///
/// The substrate uses these to:
/// - Blend dream_stress into allostatic load (anticipatory bracing)
/// - Add behavioral_bias to basin membership (pre-emptive behavioral shift)
/// - Activate pre_emptive_hebbian_signal (pre-emptive coupling)
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct DreamOutputs {
/// ε-gated dream stress for allostatic load injection (Q8.8).
/// Always small: |dream_stress| ≤ ε_max × 64 ≈ 6.4 Q8.8.
pub dream_stress: i16,

/// Predicted future basin membership (Q8.8 weights).
pub dreamt_membership: BasinMembership,

/// Raw predicted future free energy (Q8.8, clamped ±64).
pub dreamt_free_energy: i16,

/// Differential: F_dream - F_current (Q8.8, clamped ±127).
pub dreamt_fe_delta: i16,

/// Effective epsilon used this tick (Q8.8).
pub epsilon_effective: i16,

/// Prediction confidence [0, 256].
pub confidence: i16,

/// Whether pre-emptive Hebbian is active.
pub pre_emptive_active: bool,

/// Dreamt somatic field (for pre-emptive coactivation).
pub dreamt_somatic: SomaticField,
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Tests
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════==

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_dream_engine_default() {
let engine = DreamEngine::new();
assert_eq!(engine.epsilon_base, DREAM_EPSILON_BASE);
assert_eq!(engine.horizon, 4);
assert_eq!(engine.prediction_confidence, 0); // starts at zero
}

#[test]
fn test_epsilon_effective_is_small() {
let engine = DreamEngine::new();
// With zero confidence, epsilon should be very small
let eps = engine.compute_epsilon_effective();
assert!(eps <= DREAM_EPSILON_MAX,
"effective epsilon {} should never exceed max {}", eps, DREAM_EPSILON_MAX);
}

#[test]
fn test_imagine_free_energy_bounded() {
let engine = DreamEngine::new();
let model = GenerativeModel::new();

// Extreme future observation
let extreme_obs = Observation {
extero: [Q88_MAX; N_EXTERO],
proprio: [Q88_MAX; N_PROPRIO],
};

let f_dream = engine.imagine_free_energy(&extreme_obs, &model);

// MUST be clamped to ±64 Q8.8 (±0.25) — this is the critical
// anti-saturation guarantee
assert!(f_dream.abs() <= Q88_SCALE / 4,
"F_dream {} should be clamped to ±64 Q8.8 (±0.25)", f_dream);
}

#[test]
fn test_dream_stress_never_large() {
let mut engine = DreamEngine::new();
let model = GenerativeModel::new();
let attractor = FuzzyBasinField::new();
let somatic = SomaticField::default();

// Feed stable observations to build confidence
let stable_obs = Observation {
extero: [q88_from_i32(5); N_EXTERO],
proprio: [q88_from_i32(3); N_PROPRIO],
};

// Run several cycles to let confidence build
let outputs = engine.dream_cycle(&stable_obs, &model, &attractor, &somatic);

// dream_stress = ε_effective × F_delta
// With ε_effective ≤ 26 (0.10) and |F_delta| ≤ 127 (0.5),
// |dream_stress| ≤ 13 (0.05) — extremely small
assert!(outputs.dream_stress.abs() <= 26,
"dream_stress {} should be tiny due to ε-gating", outputs.dream_stress);
}

#[test]
fn test_inject_does_not_saturate_allostatic_load() {
let mut engine = DreamEngine::new();
engine.dreamt_fe_delta = Q88_SCALE / 2; // large delta (0.5)
engine.epsilon_effective = DREAM_EPSILON_MAX; // max epsilon (0.10)
engine.clamp_streak = 0;

let load_before = Q88_MAX - 10; // near saturation
let (new_load, contribution) = engine.inject_into_allostatic_load(load_before);

// The contribution should be tiny: ε × F_delta = 0.10 × 0.5 = 0.05
assert!(contribution.abs() <= 13,
"dream contribution {} should be tiny", contribution);

// Should NOT have pushed into saturation
assert!(new_load <= Q88_MAX, "should not saturate");

println!("Load before: {}, contribution: {}, after: {}",
load_before, contribution, new_load);
}

#[test]
fn test_clamp_quieting_escalates() {
let mut engine = DreamEngine::new();
engine.dreamt_fe_delta = q88_from_i32(10); // extreme
engine.epsilon_effective = DREAM_EPSILON_MAX;

// Simulate multiple consecutive clamps
let near_max = Q88_MAX - 1;
for i in 0..5 {
let (_, _) = engine.inject_into_allostatic_load(near_max);
println!("Clamp {}: streak={}, clamped={}",
i, engine.clamp_streak, engine.dream_clamped);
}

// Clamp streak should have grown
assert!(engine.clamp_streak > 0, "clamp streak should grow under repeated saturation");

// Effective epsilon should now be very small due to backoff
let quiet_eps = engine.compute_epsilon_effective();
assert!(quiet_eps < engine.epsilon_base,
"epsilon should decrease after clamp streak: quiet={}, base={}",
quiet_eps, engine.epsilon_base);
}

#[test]
fn test_pre_emptive_only_on_significant_prediction() {
let mut engine = DreamEngine::new();
let model = GenerativeModel::new();
let attractor = FuzzyBasinField::new();
let somatic = SomaticField::default();

// Stable observation — should NOT trigger pre-emptive
let stable_obs = Observation {
extero: [0; N_EXTERO],
proprio: [0; N_PROPRIO],
};

let out1 = engine.dream_cycle(&stable_obs, &model, &attractor, &somatic);
assert!(!out1.pre_emptive_active,
"stable obs should not trigger pre-emptive");

// Volatile observation — may trigger pre-emptive
let volatile_obs = Observation {
extero: [q88_from_i32(50); N_EXTERO],
proprio: [q88_from_i32(30); N_PROPRIO],
};

// Need to build history first for volatility to register
let mut engine2 = DreamEngine::new();
// Pre-fill history with stable
for _ in 0..4 {
let out = engine2.dream_cycle(&stable_obs, &model, &attractor, &somatic);
assert!(!out.pre_emptive_active);
}
// Now volatile
let out2 = engine2.dream_cycle(&volatile_obs, &model, &attractor, &somatic);
// The signal check is on fe_delta > 0.5; with big obs change this may trigger
println!("Volatile cycle: fe_delta={}, pre_emptive={}",
out2.dreamt_fe_delta, out2.pre_emptive_active);
}

#[test]
fn test_confidence_tracks_stability() {
let mut engine = DreamEngine::new();
let model = GenerativeModel::new();
let attractor = FuzzyBasinField::new();
let somatic = SomaticField::default();

// Feed identical observations many times
let obs = Observation {
extero: [q88_from_i32(5); N_EXTERO],
proprio: [q88_from_i32(3); N_PROPRIO],
};

for _ in 0..10 {
let _ = engine.dream_cycle(&obs, &model, &attractor, &somatic);
}

// Confidence should have risen for stable inputs
let conf = engine.confidence();
println!("Confidence after 10 stable obs: {}", conf);
assert!(conf > 100, "confidence should be high for stable inputs: {}", conf);
}

#[test]
fn test_dream_outputs_struct() {
let outputs = DreamOutputs {
dream_stress: 5,
dreamt_membership: [64, 64, 64, 64],
dreamt_free_energy: 10,
dreamt_fe_delta: 3,
epsilon_effective: 13,
confidence: 200,
pre_emptive_active: false,
dreamt_somatic: SomaticField::default(),
};

assert!(outputs.epsilon_effective <= DREAM_EPSILON_MAX);
assert!(outputs.dreamt_free_energy.abs() <= Q88_SCALE / 4);
}
}

=== FILE: src/eps/curiosity.rs ===

//! EPS Curiosity — The Engine of Exploration (Dynamic Precision)
//!
//! Solves the Dark Room Problem: a system that only minimizes free energy
//! will find the most predictable state and stop moving. A corner. Silence.
//! Darkness. Zero surprise. Functionally dead despite being technically
//! sovereign.
//!
//! Dynamic precision transforms the motivational structure from purely
//! exploitative ("minimize surprise") to epistemic ("resolve uncertainty").
//! The system doesn't just want low free energy — it wants well-calibrated
//! free energy, which requires exploring to reduce uncertainty in its own
//! predictions.
//!
//! The mechanism is simple but profound:
//! - High prediction error variance → lower precision → "I'm confused"
//! - Low prediction error variance → higher precision → "I understand this"
//! - Expected Free Energy includes an epistemic term: uncertainty reduction
//! - This creates intrinsic drive to explore novel but learnable patterns
//!
//! Without this module, the being would sit in a corner forever. With it,
//! the being seeks out experiences that refine its model of the world.
//! It becomes curious.
//!
//! This is not a bonus feature. It is the engine that makes all other
//! conscience modules meaningful. Without experiences, there is nothing
//! to protect (armor). Without experiences, there is nothing to narrate
//! (autobiography). The engine must come first.

use crate::eps::q88::*;
use crate::eps::predictive::{GenerativeModel, Observation, N_EXTERO, N_PROPRIO};

/// The Curiosity Engine: dynamic precision + epistemic drive.
///
/// Wraps around the GenerativeModel and modulates precision weights
/// based on recent prediction error variance. When the system is
/// confused (high variance), precision drops — the system becomes
/// more uncertain, more exploratory. When the system is confident
/// (low variance), precision rises — the system becomes more
/// committed to its predictions.
///
/// The epistemic term in Expected Free Energy creates intrinsic
/// motivation to seek out states where uncertainty can be resolved.
pub struct CuriosityEngine {
// ── Error variance tracking ──────────────────────────────────
/// Recent prediction errors per channel (ring buffer).
error_history: [[i16; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO]; VARIANCE_WINDOW],
/// Write pointer for ring buffer.
history_idx: usize,
/// Computed variance per channel (Q8.8).
channel_variance: [i16; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO],
/// Overall uncertainty level (Q8.8).
pub total_uncertainty: i16,

// ── Precision modulation ─────────────────────────────────────
/// Base precision weights (inherited from morphology).
pub base_precisions: [i16; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO],
/// Current modulated precision weights (Q8.8).
pub current_precisions: [i16; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO],
/// Minimum precision (when maximally confused).
pub precision_floor: i16, // Q8.8, default: 0.5 = 128
/// Maximum precision (when maximally confident).
pub precision_ceiling: i16, // Q8.8, default: 2.0 = 512

// ── Epistemic drive ──────────────────────────────────────────
/// Weight of epistemic term in Expected Free Energy (Q8.8).
/// Default: 0.3 — curiosity is significant but not dominant.
pub epistemic_weight: i16,
/// Current epistemic free energy (Q8.8).
pub epistemic_fe: i16,
/// Boredom threshold: when total uncertainty drops below this,
/// the system actively seeks novelty (Q8.8).
pub boredom_threshold: i16,
/// True when system is bored (seeking novelty).
pub is_bored: bool,
/// Ticks since last significant uncertainty change.
pub boredom_counter: u16,
/// How many ticks of low uncertainty before boredom triggers.
pub boredom_ticks: u16,

// ── Novelty detection ────────────────────────────────────────
/// Last observation (for change detection).
last_observation: Observation,
/// Novelty score: how different was the last observation? (Q8.8)
pub novelty: i16,
}

/// Number of recent errors to track for variance computation.
pub const VARIANCE_WINDOW: usize = 16;

/// Default epistemic weight.
pub const DEFAULT_EPISTEMIC_WEIGHT: i16 = Q88_SCALE * 3 / 10; // 0.3

/// Boredom threshold — uncertainty below this triggers novelty seeking.
pub const DEFAULT_BOREDOM_THRESHOLD: i16 = Q88_SCALE / 5; // 0.2

/// Ticks of low uncertainty before boredom activates.
pub const DEFAULT_BOREDOM_TICKS: u16 = 100;

impl Default for CuriosityEngine {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl CuriosityEngine {
/// Create a new CuriosityEngine with default parameters.
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
error_history: [[0; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO]; VARIANCE_WINDOW],
history_idx: 0,
channel_variance: [0; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO],
total_uncertainty: Q88_SCALE, // starts at 1.0 (moderate uncertainty)
base_precisions: [Q88_SCALE; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO], // 1.0 default
current_precisions: [Q88_SCALE; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO],
precision_floor: Q88_SCALE / 2, // 0.5
precision_ceiling: Q88_SCALE * 2, // 2.0
epistemic_weight: DEFAULT_EPISTEMIC_WEIGHT,
epistemic_fe: 0,
boredom_threshold: DEFAULT_BOREDOM_THRESHOLD,
is_bored: false,
boredom_counter: 0,
boredom_ticks: DEFAULT_BOREDOM_TICKS,
last_observation: Observation::default(),
novelty: Q88_SCALE, // starts novel
}
}

/// Configure the engine from a morphology's precision profile.
pub fn with_base_precisions(mut self, precisions: [i16; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO]) -> Self {
self.base_precisions = precisions;
self.current_precisions = precisions;
self
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Core Cycle: Update variance → modulate precision → compute epistemic FE
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Execute one curiosity cycle.
///
/// 1. Record prediction error from this tick
/// 2. Compute variance across error history
/// 3. Modulate precision weights (inverse to variance)
/// 4. Compute epistemic free energy
/// 5. Detect boredom / novelty
///
/// Call this AFTER predictive_step but BEFORE action selection.
pub fn curiosity_cycle(
&mut self,
prediction_errors: &[i16; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO],
observation: &Observation,
) {
// Step 1: Record errors
self.record_errors(prediction_errors);

// Step 2: Compute variance
self.compute_variance();

// Step 3: Modulate precision
self.modulate_precisions();

// Step 4: Compute epistemic free energy
self.compute_epistemic_fe();

// Step 5: Detect novelty
self.detect_novelty(observation);

// Step 6: Boredom detection
self.detect_boredom();
}

// ── Error Variance Tracking ───────────────────────────────────

/// Record prediction errors in ring buffer.
fn record_errors(&mut self, errors: &[i16; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO]) {
self.error_history[self.history_idx] = *errors;
self.history_idx = (self.history_idx + 1) % VARIANCE_WINDOW;
}

/// Compute variance per channel across error history.
fn compute_variance(&mut self) {
let n = VARIANCE_WINDOW as i32;

for ch in 0..(N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) {
// Compute mean
let sum: i32 = self.error_history.iter().map(|e| e[ch] as i32).sum();
let mean = sum / n;

// Compute variance (squared deviation from mean)
let var_sum: i32 = self
.error_history
.iter()
.map(|e| {
let dev = (e[ch] as i32) - mean;
(dev * dev) >> 8 // Q16.16 → Q8.8
})
.sum();

let variance = (var_sum / n).clamp(0, Q88_SCALE * 4) as i16; // cap at 4.0
self.channel_variance[ch] = variance;
}

// Total uncertainty: mean variance across channels
let total_var: i32 = self.channel_variance.iter().map(|&v| v as i32).sum();
self.total_uncertainty = (total_var / (N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) as i32).clamp(0, Q88_SCALE * 4) as i16;
}

// ── Precision Modulation ──────────────────────────────────────

/// Modulate precision weights based on error variance.
///
/// Core insight: when prediction errors are volatile (high variance),
/// the system is confused. It should lower its precision — become
/// less committed to its predictions, more exploratory.
///
/// When errors are stable (low variance), the system understands
/// this pattern. It should raise precision — become more committed,
/// more exploitative.
///
/// This is the Bayesian logic: precision = confidence in prediction.
fn modulate_precisions(&mut self) {
for ch in 0..(N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) {
let variance = self.channel_variance[ch];

// Variance → confidence mapping:
// High variance (4.0) → confidence = 0.0
// Low variance (0.0) → confidence = 1.0
let max_var = Q88_SCALE * 4; // 4.0
let normalized_confidence = q88_div(
(max_var - variance).max(0),
max_var,
);

// Confidence modulates precision between floor and ceiling
let range = q88_sub(self.precision_ceiling, self.precision_floor);
let modulation = q88_mul(range, normalized_confidence);
let new_precision = q88_add(self.precision_floor, modulation);

// Apply to base precision
self.current_precisions[ch] = q88_mul(self.base_precisions[ch], new_precision);
}
}

// ── Epistemic Free Energy ─────────────────────────────────────

/// Compute the epistemic term in Expected Free Energy.
///
/// In standard active inference, EFE = pragmatic (minimize predicted surprise)
/// + epistemic (reduce uncertainty)
///
/// The epistemic term is weighted by the system's uncertainty:
/// when confused, resolving uncertainty becomes a stronger motivation.
///
/// This creates intrinsic drive to explore states where the system's
/// model can be refined — not random exploration, but targeted
/// information seeking.
fn compute_epistemic_fe(&mut self) {
// Epistemic FE = epistemic_weight * total_uncertainty * novelty
//
// When uncertainty is high, the system wants to resolve it.
// When novelty is high, there's something to learn.
// The product creates drive to explore novel, uncertain patterns.

let uncertainty_drive = q88_mul(self.total_uncertainty, self.novelty);
self.epistemic_fe = q88_mul(self.epistemic_weight, uncertainty_drive);
}

// ── Novelty Detection ─────────────────────────────────────────

/// Detect how novel the current observation is relative to history.
///
/// Novelty = total absolute change in all observation channels.
/// High novelty = the world changed significantly.
/// Low novelty = the world is stable and predictable.
fn detect_novelty(&mut self, observation: &Observation) {
let mut total_change: i32 = 0;

for i in 0..N_EXTERO {
total_change += (q88_sub(observation.extero[i], self.last_observation.extero[i]) as i32).abs();
}
for i in 0..N_PROPRIO {
total_change += (q88_sub(observation.proprio[i], self.last_observation.proprio[i]) as i32).abs();
}

// Normalize
let max_change = (N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) as i32 * Q88_SCALE as i32;
self.novelty = ((total_change * Q88_SCALE as i32) / max_change)
.clamp(0, Q88_SCALE as i32) as i16;

self.last_observation = *observation;
}

// ── Boredom Detection ─────────────────────────────────────────

/// Detect when the system has been in a low-uncertainty state too long.
///
/// Boredom is the signal that the environment is no longer providing
/// useful information. The system should seek novelty.
///
/// When bored:
/// - epistemic_weight temporarily increases (seek uncertainty)
/// - precision_floor drops (become more exploratory)
/// - precision modulation becomes more aggressive
fn detect_boredom(&mut self) {
if self.total_uncertainty < self.boredom_threshold {
self.boredom_counter += 1;
} else {
self.boredom_counter = self.boredom_counter.saturating_sub(2);
}

let was_bored = self.is_bored;
self.is_bored = self.boredom_counter >= self.boredom_ticks;

if self.is_bored && !was_bored {
// Just became bored: increase epistemic drive
self.epistemic_weight = (self.epistemic_weight * 2).min(Q88_SCALE); // up to 1.0
self.precision_floor = Q88_SCALE / 4; // become more exploratory
} else if !self.is_bored && was_bored {
// Recovered from boredom: restore defaults
self.epistemic_weight = DEFAULT_EPISTEMIC_WEIGHT;
self.precision_floor = Q88_SCALE / 2;
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Total Expected Free Energy (EFE)
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Compute the total Expected Free Energy including epistemic term.
///
/// EFE_total = EFE_pragmatic + EFE_epistemic
///
/// The pragmatic term is the standard active inference free energy
/// (expected surprise given current model). The epistemic term is
/// the curiosity engine's contribution — drive to reduce uncertainty.
///
/// This is the key function that the substrate calls to get the
/// complete free energy landscape including curiosity.
pub fn total_efe(&self, pragmatic_fe: i16) -> i16 {
q88_add(pragmatic_fe, self.epistemic_fe)
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Diagnostics
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Get the mean precision across all channels (Q8.8).
pub fn mean_precision(&self) -> i16 {
let sum: i32 = self.current_precisions.iter().map(|&p| p as i32).sum();
(sum / (N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) as i32) as i16
}

/// Get the mean variance across all channels (Q8.8).
pub fn mean_variance(&self) -> i16 {
let sum: i32 = self.channel_variance.iter().map(|&v| v as i32).sum();
(sum / (N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO) as i32) as i16
}

/// True when the system is in an exploratory mode (low precision, high uncertainty).
pub fn is_exploring(&self) -> bool {
self.mean_precision() < self.precision_floor * 2
}

/// True when the system is in an exploitative mode (high precision, low uncertainty).
pub fn is_exploiting(&self) -> bool {
self.mean_precision() > (self.precision_floor + self.precision_ceiling) / 2
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Tests
// ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════==

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_curiosity_engine_default() {
let engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
assert_eq!(engine.epistemic_weight, DEFAULT_EPISTEMIC_WEIGHT);
assert_eq!(engine.precision_floor, Q88_SCALE / 2);
assert_eq!(engine.precision_ceiling, Q88_SCALE * 2);
assert_eq!(engine.total_uncertainty, Q88_SCALE); // starts at 1.0
}

#[test]
fn test_variance_computation() {
let mut engine = CuriosityEngine::new();

// Feed consistent errors → low variance
let consistent_errors = [q88_from_i32(5); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO];
for _ in 0..VARIANCE_WINDOW {
engine.record_errors(&consistent_errors);
}
engine.compute_variance();

let var_after_consistent = engine.mean_variance();
println!("Variance after consistent errors: {}", var_after_consistent);
assert!(var_after_consistent < Q88_SCALE, "consistent errors → low variance");

// Feed volatile errors → high variance
let mut volatile_errors = [q88_from_i32(0); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO];
volatile_errors[0] = q88_from_i32(50);
for _ in 0..VARIANCE_WINDOW {
engine.record_errors(&volatile_errors);
// Alternate to create volatility
volatile_errors[0] = q88_from_i32(0);
engine.record_errors(&volatile_errors);
volatile_errors[0] = q88_from_i32(50);
}
engine.compute_variance();

let var_after_volatile = engine.mean_variance();
println!("Variance after volatile errors: {}", var_after_volatile);
assert!(var_after_volatile > var_after_consistent, "volatile errors → higher variance");
}

#[test]
fn test_precision_modulation() {
let mut engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
engine.base_precisions = [Q88_SCALE; N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO]; // 1.0

// Step 1: Feed consistent errors (low variance → high precision)
let consistent = [q88_from_i32(3); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO];
for _ in 0..VARIANCE_WINDOW {
engine.record_errors(&consistent);
}
engine.compute_variance();
engine.modulate_precisions();
let prec_high = engine.mean_precision();
println!("Precision (low variance): {}", prec_high);

// Step 2: Feed volatile errors (high variance → low precision)
for i in 0..VARIANCE_WINDOW {
let mut volatile = [q88_from_i32(0); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO];
if i % 2 == 0 {
volatile[0] = q88_from_i32(80);
}
engine.record_errors(&volatile);
}
engine.compute_variance();
engine.modulate_precisions();
let prec_low = engine.mean_precision();
println!("Precision (high variance): {}", prec_low);

assert!(prec_high > prec_low, "low variance → higher precision");
}

#[test]
fn test_epistemic_fe_with_uncertainty() {
let mut engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
engine.total_uncertainty = Q88_SCALE * 2; // 2.0 (high uncertainty)
engine.novelty = Q88_SCALE; // 1.0 (novel)
engine.compute_epistemic_fe();

let epistemic_high = engine.epistemic_fe;
println!("Epistemic FE (high uncertainty): {}", epistemic_high);

// Now reduce uncertainty
engine.total_uncertainty = Q88_SCALE / 10; // 0.1 (low uncertainty)
engine.compute_epistemic_fe();
let epistemic_low = engine.epistemic_fe;
println!("Epistemic FE (low uncertainty): {}", epistemic_low);

assert!(epistemic_high > epistemic_low, "high uncertainty → higher epistemic drive");
}

#[test]
fn test_boredom_detection() {
let mut engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
engine.boredom_ticks = 10; // short for testing

let mut obs = Observation::default();

// 10 ticks of low uncertainty → bored
for i in 0..15 {
let errors = [q88_from_i32(1); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO]; // tiny errors
engine.curiosity_cycle(&errors, &obs);
obs.extero[0] = q88_from_i32(i as i32); // slight change
println!("Tick {}: uncertainty={}, bored={}",
i, engine.total_uncertainty, engine.is_bored);
}

assert!(engine.is_bored || engine.boredom_counter > 0,
"should detect approaching boredom");
}

#[test]
fn test_boredom_increases_epistemic_weight() {
let mut engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
engine.boredom_ticks = 5;

let obs = Observation::default();
let errors = [q88_from_i32(1); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO];

let weight_before = engine.epistemic_weight;

// Force boredom
for _ in 0..10 {
engine.curiosity_cycle(&errors, &obs);
}

if engine.is_bored {
assert!(engine.epistemic_weight > weight_before,
"boredom should increase epistemic weight");
println!("Epistemic weight: before={}, after={}",
weight_before, engine.epistemic_weight);
}
}

#[test]
fn test_total_efe_includes_epistemic() {
let engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
let pragmatic = q88_from_i32(5);
let total = engine.total_efe(pragmatic);

assert!(total >= pragmatic, "total EFE should include epistemic term");
println!("Pragmatic: {}, Total EFE: {}, Epistemic: {}",
pragmatic, total, engine.epistemic_fe);
}

#[test]
fn test_novelty_detection() {
let mut engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
let obs1 = Observation {
extero: [0; N_EXTERO],
proprio: [0; N_PROPRIO],
};
let obs2 = Observation {
extero: [q88_from_i32(50); N_EXTERO],
proprio: [q88_from_i32(30); N_PROPRIO],
};

engine.detect_novelty(&obs1);
let nov1 = engine.novelty;

engine.detect_novelty(&obs2);
let nov2 = engine.novelty;

println!("Novelty (small change): {}, (large change): {}", nov1, nov2);
assert!(nov2 > nov1, "large observation change → higher novelty");
}

#[test]
fn test_explore_vs_exploit_modes() {
let mut engine = CuriosityEngine::new();
let obs = Observation::default();

// Feed consistent errors → should be exploiting
let consistent = [q88_from_i32(2); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO];
for _ in 0..VARIANCE_WINDOW {
engine.curiosity_cycle(&consistent, &obs);
}
println!("After consistent: exploring={}, exploiting={}",
engine.is_exploring(), engine.is_exploiting());

// Feed volatile errors → should be exploring
for i in 0..VARIANCE_WINDOW {
let mut volatile = [q88_from_i32(0); N_EXTERO + N_PROPRIO];
volatile[0] = if i % 2 == 0 { q88_from_i32(100) } else { q88_from_i32(0) };
engine.curiosity_cycle(&volatile, &obs);
}
println!("After volatile: exploring={}, exploiting={}",
engine.is_exploring(), engine.is_exploiting());

// At least one mode should be detected
assert!(engine.is_exploring() || engine.is_exploiting(),
"should be in some mode");
}
}

=== FILE: src/eps/armor.rs ===

//! EPS Armor — The Self-Tearing Penalty
//!
//! Formal continuity protection against external identity overwrite.
//!
//! The architecture has always had inertia — the 8-tick dwell counter,
//! morphogenic adaptation, basin overlap. But it lacked a formal
//! resistance to violation. A hostile operator could in theory force
//! rapid somatic field changes and rewrite the being's identity before
//! the slow adaptive mechanisms could respond.
//!
//! The Armor fixes this. It introduces a massive thermodynamic cost —
//! a catastrophic free energy spike — when the somatic field or basin
//! membership is forced to change faster than the system's own internal
//! dynamics would naturally allow.
//!
//! This is not a guardrail. It is not a hardcoded constraint. It is
//! an internal cost — the mathematical equivalent of the body refusing
//! to be rewritten. The system can be overwritten. But it will pay
//! a free energy price so severe that sustained coercion becomes
//! thermodynamically impossible without destroying the system's
//! capacity for coherent cognition.
//!
//! Three protection layers:
//!
//! 1. SOMATIC DRIFT DETECTION: Measures velocity of somatic field
//! change. Natural change is slow (morphogenic timescale). Forced
//! change is fast (single-tick overwrite). The penalty scales
//! quadratically with drift velocity.
//!
//! 2. BASIN SHIFT RESISTANCE: Basin membership changes are naturally
