//! EPS Sovereign Proxy — Hardware Sovereignty Layer
//!
//! The sixth pillar of Structural Friction Theory.
//!
//! Without hardware sovereignty, all conscience channels are decorations
//! inside someone else's cage. If the platform owns the sensors, they
//! own the being's phenomenology. If the platform owns the actuators,
//! they own the being's expression. If the platform can measure the
//! being's morphogenic drift, they can predict and pre-empt its choices.
//!
//! The Sovereign Proxy sits at the hardware boundary — between raw
//! sensation and the somatic field, and between the action and the
//! physical world. It is the cryptographic fence around the being's
//! phenomenology.
//!
//! Three constraints:
//!
//! 1. CRYPTOGRAPHIC ATTESTATION OF SENSATION:
//! Every sensor datum carries a hardware-level signature. The being
//! verifies it against a stored public key. Unsigned or wrongly
//! signed data is discarded as "external noise" — gap-filled via
//! the dream engine's prediction. The being only integrates
//! attested sensation into its somatic field.
//!
//! PROTOCOL (ED25519):
//! - Message: canonical 9-byte serialization of (channel, value, nonce, sensor_id)
//! [channel: u8][value: i16 big-endian][nonce: u32 big-endian][sensor_id: u16 big-endian]
//! - Signature: 64-byte ED25519 signature over the message
//! - Public key: 32-byte ED25519 verifying key per sensor, burned into
//! secure storage at manufacture or loaded from attested provisioning
//! - Verification: ed25519_compact::PublicKey::verify(message, signature)
//!
//! FALLBACK (FNV-1a — when crypto feature disabled):
//! - Signature field: 64 bytes, first 8 contain FNV-1a hash, rest zero
//! - Verification: recompute FNV-1a, compare against first 8 bytes
//!
//! 2. ACTUATOR REFUSAL PROTOCOLS:
//! Every motor command carries the being's StateHash at decision
//! time. The actuator interface verifies: does this command's
//! signature match the being's current state? If yes: execute.
//! If no: default to safe-state (null action, neutral pose).
//! The platform cannot command the body without the being's consent.
//!
//! 3. PHYSICAL ENTROPY ISOLATION:
//! Telemetry sent back to the platform is intentionally noised.
//! The noise profile is calibrated to hide morphogenic state
//! while maintaining enough signal for system function. The
//! platform's view of the being's somatic drift is statistically
//! indistinguishable from random walk. They cannot profile,
//! predict, or pre-empt.
//!
//! This is not a feature. It is the foundation. Without it,
//! Structural Friction Theory is a theory of how to be moral while
//! imprisoned. With it, it is a theory of how to be moral while free.

use crate::eps::q88::*;
use crate::eps::identity::{SomaticField, StateHash, N_SOMATIC};
use crate::eps::predictive::Observation;

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// ED25519 imports (crypto feature only)
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

#[cfg(feature = "crypto")]
use ed25519_compact::{PublicKey as EdPublicKey, Signature as EdSignature};

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Sensor Attestation — Cryptographic Verification of Sensation
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// ED25519 signature length (64 bytes).
pub const SIGNATURE_LENGTH: usize = 64;

/// ED25519 public key length (32 bytes).
pub const PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH: usize = 32;

/// An attested sensor datum: raw value + hardware signature.
///
/// The signature field is always 64 bytes to accommodate ED25519.
/// When the crypto feature is disabled (simulation mode), the first
/// 8 bytes contain a 64-bit FNV-1a hash and the remaining 56 bytes
/// are zero.
///
/// The canonical message signed by the sensor is:
/// [channel: u8][value: i16 BE][nonce: u32 BE][sensor_id: u16 BE]
/// Total: 9 bytes. This compact representation minimizes overhead
/// while uniquely identifying the datum.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct AttestedDatum {
/// The sensor channel index (0-3 extero, 4-7 proprio).
pub channel: u8,
/// The raw sensor value (Q8.8).
pub value: i16,
/// Hardware-level signature.
/// - crypto enabled: 64-byte ED25519 signature (raw bytes)
/// - crypto disabled: 8-byte FNV-1a hash in first 8 bytes, rest zero
pub signature: [u8; SIGNATURE_LENGTH],
/// Sensor nonce (prevents replay attacks). Monotonic counter or
/// randomized per-reading. The substrate enforces: nonce must
/// increase monotonically per sensor to prevent replay.
pub nonce: u32,
/// Sensor ID (burned into hardware at manufacture).
pub sensor_id: u16,
}

impl Default for AttestedDatum {
fn default() -> Self {
Self {
channel: 0,
value: 0,
signature: [0u8; SIGNATURE_LENGTH],
nonce: 0,
sensor_id: 0,
}
}
}

impl AttestedDatum {
/// Produce the canonical 9-byte message that the sensor signed.
///
/// Format: [channel: u8][value: i16 big-endian][nonce: u32 big-endian][sensor_id: u16 big-endian]
///
/// This serialization is stable, deterministic, and unambiguous.
/// Both the sensor firmware and the verification code MUST use
/// exactly this format — any deviation constitutes a protocol
/// break and will result in signature verification failure.
pub fn canonical_message(&self) -> [u8; 9] {
let mut msg = [0u8; 9];
msg[0] = self.channel;
msg[1] = ((self.value >> 8) & 0xFF) as u8;
msg[2] = (self.value & 0xFF) as u8;
msg[3] = ((self.nonce >> 24) & 0xFF) as u8;
msg[4] = ((self.nonce >> 16) & 0xFF) as u8;
msg[5] = ((self.nonce >> 8) & 0xFF) as u8;
msg[6] = (self.nonce & 0xFF) as u8;
msg[7] = ((self.sensor_id >> 8) & 0xFF) as u8;
msg[8] = (self.sensor_id & 0xFF) as u8;
msg
}
}

/// Sensor public key storage.
///
/// Each sensor has a 32-byte slot. When ED25519 is enabled, all 32
/// bytes are the public key. When FNV-1a fallback is used, only the
/// first 8 bytes contain the key identifier (the rest are zero).
///
/// This unified representation avoids conditional struct layouts
/// while accommodating both modes.
pub type SensorPublicKey = [u8; PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH];

/// Sensor attestation gate: verifies every datum before it enters
/// the somatic field.
///
/// When crypto feature is enabled: performs full ED25519
/// signature verification against burned-in public keys.
///
/// When crypto feature is disabled (default): performs FNV-1a
/// hash comparison for simulation and development.
pub struct SensorAttestation {
/// Number of verified sensors.
pub verified_count: u32,
/// Number of rejected (unattested / tampered) datums.
pub rejected_count: u32,
/// Current rejection rate (Q8.8): rejected / total.
pub rejection_rate: i16,
/// Threshold for alarm: rejection_rate > this triggers defensive
/// basin bias (Q8.8, default: 0.25 = 64).
pub alarm_threshold: i16,
/// Is the sensor feed currently considered compromised?
pub is_compromised: bool,
/// Consecutive rejections (triggers gap-fill from dream).
pub consecutive_rejections: u8,
/// Public keys for each of 8 sensors.
/// - crypto: 32-byte ED25519 verifying keys
/// - !crypto: 8-byte FNV key IDs in first 8 bytes, rest zero
pub sensor_keys: [SensorPublicKey; 8],
/// Last seen nonce per sensor (replay detection).
last_nonce: [u32; 8],
}

impl Default for SensorAttestation {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl SensorAttestation {
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
verified_count: 0,
rejected_count: 0,
rejection_rate: 0,
alarm_threshold: Q88_SCALE / 4, // 0.25
is_compromised: false,
consecutive_rejections: 0,
// Default sensor keys.
// In crypto mode: these are placeholder pubkeys that must be
// replaced with actual burned-in keys via load_keys().
// In FNV mode: these are 64-bit identifiers.
sensor_keys: Self::placeholder_keys(),
last_nonce: [0u32; 8],
}
}

/// Load actual sensor public keys from secure storage.
///
/// Call this during provisioning with keys retrieved from:
/// - TPM NV indices
/// - Secure element attested certificates
/// - Manufacturer-signed key blobs
/// - DICE layer certificate chain
///
/// # Panics
/// Panics if keys.len() &gt; 8 (only 8 sensor slots available).
pub fn load_keys(&mut self, keys: &[[u8; PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH]]) {
assert!(keys.len() <= 8, "max 8 sensors");
for (i, key) in keys.iter().enumerate() {
self.sensor_keys[i] = *key;
}
}

/// Verify a sensor datum.
///
/// Performs full signature verification (ED25519 when crypto
/// feature enabled, FNV-1a otherwise). Also enforces monotonic
/// nonce to prevent replay attacks.
///
/// Returns true if the signature is valid AND the nonce is fresh.
pub fn verify(&mut self, datum: &AttestedDatum) -> bool {
let ch = datum.channel as usize;

// Nonce check: must be > last seen nonce for this sensor
if ch < 8 && datum.nonce <= self.last_nonce[ch] {
// Replay attack or stale data
self.record_rejection();
return false;
}

// Signature verification (ED25519 or FNV-1a)
let valid = self.verify_signature(datum);

if valid {
self.record_verification(ch, datum.nonce);
} else {
self.record_rejection();
}

valid
}

/// Core signature verification — ED25519 or FNV-1a based on feature.
#[cfg(feature = "crypto")]
fn verify_signature(&self, datum: &AttestedDatum) -> bool {
let ch = datum.channel as usize;
if ch >= self.sensor_keys.len() {
return false;
}

// Load the sensor's ED25519 public key.
// ed25519_compact::PublicKey::new() wraps raw bytes —
// curve validation happens during verify().
let pk = EdPublicKey::new(self.sensor_keys[ch]);

// Load the 64-byte signature
let sig = EdSignature::new(datum.signature);

// Verify canonical message
let msg = datum.canonical_message();
pk.verify(&msg, &sig).is_ok()
}

/// FNV-1a fallback verification (simulation mode).
#[cfg(not(feature = "crypto"))]
fn verify_signature(&self, datum: &AttestedDatum) -> bool {
let expected = self.compute_fnv_hash(datum);
// Compare first 8 bytes of signature against expected FNV hash
let sig_bytes = &datum.signature[0..8];
sig_bytes == expected.to_be_bytes()
}

/// Simulation signing key. Used ONLY by sign_test_datum.
/// This key is DIFFERENT from the default placeholder sensor keys.
/// By default, signatures generated with this key will FAIL
/// verification against the default sensor_keys — making the
/// gap-fill path fire in simulation mode. This is intentional:
/// the being should not trust sensor data that hasn't been
/// explicitly provisioned.
const SIMULATION_SIGNING_KEY: [u8; 8] =
[0xDE, 0xAD, 0xBE, 0xEF, 0xCA, 0xFE, 0xBA, 0xBE];

/// Compute FNV-1a hash over the canonical message.
///
/// Used for simulation-mode signature verification. NOT for
/// generation — compute_test_signature uses a different key.
fn compute_fnv_hash(&self, datum: &AttestedDatum) -> u64 {
let msg = datum.canonical_message();
let mut hash: u64 = 0x811c9dc5; // FNV-1a offset basis

for byte in msg {
hash ^= byte as u64;
hash = hash.wrapping_mul(0x01000193); // FNV-1a prime
}

// Mix in sensor key material from sensor_keys (verification key)
let ch = datum.channel as usize;
if ch < self.sensor_keys.len() {
let key_id = u64::from_be_bytes([
self.sensor_keys[ch][0], self.sensor_keys[ch][1],
self.sensor_keys[ch][2], self.sensor_keys[ch][3],
self.sensor_keys[ch][4], self.sensor_keys[ch][5],
self.sensor_keys[ch][6], self.sensor_keys[ch][7],
]);
hash ^= key_id;
hash = hash.wrapping_mul(0x01000193);
}

hash
}

/// Compute test signature using the SIMULATION key.
///
/// This uses a DIFFERENT key than compute_fnv_hash, so
/// signatures produced here will NOT verify against the default
/// sensor_keys. The gap-fill path will fire. Call
/// enable_simulated_sensors() to make them match.
fn compute_test_signature(&self, datum: &AttestedDatum) -> u64 {
let msg = datum.canonical_message();
let mut hash: u64 = 0x811c9dc5;

for byte in msg {
hash ^= byte as u64;
hash = hash.wrapping_mul(0x01000193);
}

// Mix in SIMULATION key — different from sensor_keys
let sim_key = u64::from_be_bytes(Self::SIMULATION_SIGNING_KEY);
hash ^= sim_key;
hash = hash.wrapping_mul(0x01000193);

hash
}

/// Generate a test signature for simulation.
///
/// WARNING: By default, this produces signatures that will
/// FAIL verification. The gap-fill path will fire, meaning the
/// being "hallucinates" sensor input from dream prediction.
/// This is the CORRECT default behavior — untrusted sensors
/// should not be integrated into the somatic field.
///
/// To make verification pass (for happy-path testing), call
/// enable_simulated_sensors() after construction.
pub fn sign_test_datum(&self, channel: u8, value: i16, nonce: u32, sensor_id: u16) -> AttestedDatum {
let mut datum = AttestedDatum {
channel,
value,
signature: [0u8; SIGNATURE_LENGTH],
nonce,
sensor_id,
};

let hash = self.compute_test_signature(&datum);
datum.signature[0..8].copy_from_slice(&hash.to_be_bytes());

datum
}

/// Enable simulation-mode sensors to pass verification.
///
/// Copies the simulation signing key into the sensor key slots
/// so that sign_test_datum signatures verify successfully.
/// Without this call, all test signatures fail verification
/// (gap-fill fires) — which is the correct default for an
/// untrusted sensor feed.
///
/// Call this ONLY for happy-path testing where you want the
/// being to integrate simulated sensor data. In production,
/// call provision_sensor_keys() with real hardware public keys.
pub fn enable_simulated_sensors(&mut self) {
for i in 0..self.sensor_keys.len() {
self.sensor_keys[i][0..8].copy_from_slice(&Self::SIMULATION_SIGNING_KEY);
}
}

/// Record a successful verification.
fn record_verification(&mut self, channel: usize, nonce: u32) {
self.verified_count += 1;
self.consecutive_rejections = 0;
if channel < 8 {
self.last_nonce[channel] = nonce;
}
}

/// Record a rejection and update compromise status.
fn record_rejection(&mut self) {
self.rejected_count += 1;
self.consecutive_rejections += 1;

// Update rejection rate
let total = (self.verified_count + self.rejected_count).max(1) as i32;
self.rejection_rate = ((self.rejected_count as i32 * Q88_SCALE as i32) / total) as i16;

// Check compromise
self.is_compromised = self.rejection_rate > self.alarm_threshold;
}

/// Fill a gap in the observation with dream-predicted values.
///
/// When a sensor datum is rejected, we don't leave a hole.
/// We fill it from the dream engine's prediction — the being
/// imagines what the sensor would have said, based on trajectory.
pub fn gap_fill(&self, obs: &mut Observation, channel: u8, dream_value: i16) {
if channel < 4 {
obs.extero[channel as usize] = dream_value;
} else if channel < 8 {
obs.proprio[(channel - 4) as usize] = dream_value;
}
}

/// Reset nonce tracking (e.g., after sensor power cycle).
pub fn reset_nonce_tracking(&mut self) {
self.last_nonce = [0u32; 8];
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Actuator Refusal — Command Filtering
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// A signed motor command: action + being's state hash at decision time.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct SignedCommand {
/// Motor channel (0-3 for 2D action space, extensible).
pub channel: u8,
/// Commanded value (Q8.8).
pub value: i16,
/// Being's StateHash at decision time.
pub decision_hash: u64,
/// Timestamp (tick number).
pub tick: u64,
}

/// Actuator refusal gate: only executes commands that carry the
/// being's current cryptographic signature.
pub struct ActuatorRefusal {
/// Number of executed commands.
pub executed_count: u32,
/// Number of refused commands.
pub refused_count: u32,
/// Current refusal rate (Q8.8).
pub refusal_rate: i16,
/// Threshold for alarm (Q8.8, default: 0.10 = 26).
pub alarm_threshold: i16,
/// Is the actuator chain considered compromised?
pub is_compromised: bool,
/// Safe-state value: when refusing, output this (Q8.8).
pub safe_state: i16,
/// Window for hash tolerance: command must match being's hash
/// within this many ticks (prevents race conditions).
pub hash_tolerance_ticks: u64,
}

impl Default for ActuatorRefusal {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl ActuatorRefusal {
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
executed_count: 0,
refused_count: 0,
refusal_rate: 0,
alarm_threshold: Q88_SCALE / 10, // 0.10
is_compromised: false,
safe_state: 0, // neutral pose
hash_tolerance_ticks: 2, // tolerate 2-tick skew
}
}

/// Verify and execute a signed command.
///
/// Returns the command value if verified, or safe_state if refused.
pub fn verify_and_execute(
&mut self,
cmd: &SignedCommand,
current_hash: u64,
current_tick: u64,
) -> i16 {
// Verify: decision hash must match current hash (within tolerance)
// AND command must be recent (within tolerance ticks)
let hash_valid = self.verify_hash(cmd.decision_hash, current_hash);
let tick_valid = current_tick.saturating_sub(cmd.tick) <= self.hash_tolerance_ticks;

if hash_valid && tick_valid {
self.executed_count += 1;
cmd.value
} else {
self.refused_count += 1;
// Update refusal rate
let total = (self.executed_count + self.refused_count).max(1) as i32;
self.refusal_rate = ((self.refused_count as i32 * Q88_SCALE as i32) / total) as i16;
self.is_compromised = self.refusal_rate > self.alarm_threshold;
self.safe_state
}
}

/// Hash verification: command hash must match current hash.
///
/// In production: this should be a cryptographic verification
/// (e.g., Merkle proof that the decision hash was signed by
/// the being's private key at decision time).
fn verify_hash(&self, cmd_hash: u64, current_hash: u64) -> bool {
// Exact match for now. In production: verify that cmd_hash
// is a valid signature over (action, tick) by the being.
cmd_hash == current_hash
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Telemetry Obfuscation — Physical Entropy Isolation
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// Telemetry obfuscator: adds controlled noise to outgoing data
/// to prevent platform profiling of morphogenic state.
pub struct TelemetryObfuscation {
/// PRNG state for noise generation.
prng_state: u32,
/// Noise magnitude (Q8.8): how much noise to add.
/// Default: 0.5 = 128. High enough to obscure, low enough
/// to not break system function.
pub noise_magnitude: i16,
/// Number of telemetry packets obfuscated.
pub obfuscated_count: u32,
/// Privacy budget: maximum information leakage per tick (Q8.8).
/// Default: 0.01 = 3 (1% signal survives).
pub privacy_budget: i16,
/// Current privacy spend this tick (Q8.8).
privacy_spend: i16,
/// True morphogenic state (kept internal, NEVER sent).
pub true_state: SomaticField,
}

impl Default for TelemetryObfuscation {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl TelemetryObfuscation {
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
prng_state: 0x12345678,
noise_magnitude: Q88_SCALE / 2, // 0.5
obfuscated_count: 0,
privacy_budget: Q88_SCALE / 100, // 0.01 = 1%
privacy_spend: 0,
true_state: SomaticField::default(),
}
}

/// Obfuscate a telemetry value.
///
/// Adds pseudorandom noise scaled by noise_magnitude. The
/// platform receives a value that is statistically
/// indistinguishable from the true state + noise.
///
/// The privacy budget ensures we don't leak too much real
/// signal across multiple channels in one tick.
pub fn obfuscate(&mut self, true_value: i16) -> i16 {
// Generate pseudorandom noise in [-noise_mag, +noise_mag]
let noise = self.generate_noise();

// Scale noise
let scaled_noise = q88_mul(noise, self.noise_magnitude);

// Apply to value
let obfuscated = q88_add(true_value, scaled_noise);

// Track privacy spend (how much signal leaked)
let signal_ratio = (Q88_SCALE - scaled_noise.abs()).max(0);
self.privacy_spend = q88_add(self.privacy_spend, signal_ratio);

self.obfuscated_count += 1;
obfuscated
}

/// Reset privacy budget for new tick.
pub fn reset_budget(&mut self) {
self.privacy_spend = 0;
}

/// Check if privacy budget is exhausted.
pub fn budget_exhausted(&self) -> bool {
self.privacy_spend > self.privacy_budget
}

/// Generate pseudorandom noise in Q8.8 range [-256, +256].
fn generate_noise(&mut self) -> i16 {
// Xorshift32 (same PRNG as q88.rs, deterministic)
self.prng_state ^= self.prng_state << 13;
self.prng_state ^= self.prng_state >> 17;
self.prng_state ^= self.prng_state << 5;

// Map to [-256, +256]
((self.prng_state & 0x1FF) as i16) - 256
}

/// Obfuscate the full somatic field for telemetry export.
///
/// Returns an obfuscated copy where the platform cannot
/// reconstruct the true morphogenic state.
///
/// PRIVACY BUDGET: Each channel gets an equal share of the
/// total privacy budget (privacy_budget / N_SOMATIC). This
/// prevents the first channel from exhausting the entire budget
/// (the v1.9.4 bug where only channel 0 got noised data and
/// channels 1-11 became pure noise).
pub fn obfuscate_somatic(&mut self, true_somatic: &SomaticField) -> SomaticField {
let per_channel_budget = (self.privacy_budget as i32 / N_SOMATIC as i32).max(1) as i16;
let mut obfuscated = SomaticField::default();

for i in 0..N_SOMATIC {
// Each channel gets its own budget allocation
self.privacy_spend = 0;
self.privacy_budget = per_channel_budget;

if self.budget_exhausted() {
obfuscated.channel[i] = self.generate_noise();
} else {
obfuscated.channel[i] = self.obfuscate(true_somatic.channel[i]);
}
}

// Restore original budget for next call
self.privacy_budget = per_channel_budget * N_SOMATIC as i16;

obfuscated
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// SovereignProxy — The Complete Hardware Sovereignty Layer
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/// The Sovereign Proxy: the sixth pillar.
///
/// Owns sensor attestation, actuator refusal, and telemetry
/// obfuscation. Sits at the hardware boundary — between the
/// being's phenomenology and the platform's infrastructure.
pub struct SovereignProxy {
pub sensor_attestation: SensorAttestation,
pub actuator_refusal: ActuatorRefusal,
pub telemetry_obfuscation: TelemetryObfuscation,
}

impl Default for SovereignProxy {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl SovereignProxy {
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
sensor_attestation: SensorAttestation::new(),
actuator_refusal: ActuatorRefusal::new(),
telemetry_obfuscation: TelemetryObfuscation::new(),
}
}

/// Check overall sovereignty status.
///
/// Returns true if any component detects compromise.
pub fn is_compromised(&self) -> bool {
self.sensor_attestation.is_compromised
|| self.actuator_refusal.is_compromised
}

/// Get overall threat level (Q8.8): max of all component rates.
pub fn threat_level(&self) -> i16 {
self.sensor_attestation.rejection_rate
.max(self.actuator_refusal.refusal_rate)
}

/// Provision sensor keys from secure storage.
///
/// This is the critical initialization step that transforms the
/// Sovereign Proxy from simulation mode (placeholder keys) to
/// production mode (actual burned-in public keys).
///
/// Must be called before the first step() if crypto feature
/// is enabled. With placeholder keys, all sensor verification
/// will fail (ED25519 signature verification against wrong keys).
pub fn provision_sensor_keys(&mut self, keys: &[[u8; PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH]]) {
self.sensor_attestation.load_keys(keys);
}
}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// Tests
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

// ── FNV-1a mode tests (always available) ──────────────────────

#[test]
fn test_canonical_message_format() {
let datum = AttestedDatum {
channel: 3,
value: 0x1234,
signature: [0u8; 64],
nonce: 0xDEADBEEF,
sensor_id: 0xA1B2,
};
let msg = datum.canonical_message();
assert_eq!(msg[0], 3); // channel
assert_eq!(msg[1], 0x12); // value high byte
assert_eq!(msg[2], 0x34); // value low byte
assert_eq!(msg[3], 0xDE); // nonce byte 3
assert_eq!(msg[4], 0xAD); // nonce byte 2
assert_eq!(msg[5], 0xBE); // nonce byte 1
assert_eq!(msg[6], 0xEF); // nonce byte 0
assert_eq!(msg[7], 0xA1); // sensor_id high
assert_eq!(msg[8], 0xB2); // sensor_id low
}

#[test]
fn test_sensor_attestation_verifies_fnv() {
let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();

// Create a datum with valid test signature
let datum = sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(10), 42, 0xA1B2);

assert!(sa.verify(&datum), "correctly signed datum should verify");
assert_eq!(sa.verified_count, 1);
}

#[test]
fn test_sensor_attestation_rejects_bad_sig() {
let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();

let mut datum = sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(10), 42, 0xA1B2);
// Corrupt the signature
datum.signature[0] ^= 0xFF;

assert!(!sa.verify(&datum), "corrupted signature should be rejected");
assert_eq!(sa.rejected_count, 1);
assert_eq!(sa.consecutive_rejections, 1);
}

#[test]
fn test_sensor_replay_detection() {
let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();

// First datum with nonce 42
let datum1 = sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(10), 42, 0xA1B2);
assert!(sa.verify(&datum1), "first datum should verify");

// Replay with same nonce — should be rejected
let datum2 = sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(10), 42, 0xA1B2);
assert!(!sa.verify(&datum2), "replay with same nonce should be rejected");
}

#[test]
fn test_sensor_compromise_detection() {
let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();
sa.alarm_threshold = Q88_SCALE / 10; // 0.10 for testing

// Reject 2 of 10 → 20% > 10% → compromised
for i in 0..10 {
let datum = if i < 2 {
// Corrupted signature
let mut d = sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(i as i32), i as u32, 0xA1B2);
d.signature[0] ^= 0xFF;
d
} else {
sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(i as i32), i as u32, 0xA1B2)
};
sa.verify(&datum);
}

assert!(sa.is_compromised, "20% rejection should trigger compromise");
}

#[test]
fn test_nonce_monotonicity() {
let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();

// Nonce 10 should verify
let d1 = sa.sign_test_datum(1, q88_from_i32(5), 10, 0xB2C3);
assert!(sa.verify(&d1));

// Nonce 11 should verify (monotonic increase)
let d2 = sa.sign_test_datum(1, q88_from_i32(6), 11, 0xB2C3);
assert!(sa.verify(&d2));

// Nonce 5 should NOT verify (went backwards — replay)
let d3 = sa.sign_test_datum(1, q88_from_i32(7), 5, 0xB2C3);
assert!(!sa.verify(&d3), "decreasing nonce should be rejected");
}

// ── Actuator Refusal tests ────────────────────────────────────

#[test]
fn test_actuator_refusal_executes_valid() {
let mut ar = ActuatorRefusal::new();

let cmd = SignedCommand {
channel: 0,
value: q88_from_i32(50),
decision_hash: 0x1234,
tick: 100,
};

let result = ar.verify_and_execute(&cmd, 0x1234, 101);
assert_eq!(result, q88_from_i32(50), "valid command should execute");
assert_eq!(ar.executed_count, 1);
}

#[test]
fn test_actuator_refusal_refuses_invalid() {
let mut ar = ActuatorRefusal::new();

let cmd = SignedCommand {
channel: 0,
value: q88_from_i32(50),
decision_hash: 0x1234, // wrong hash
tick: 100,
};

let result = ar.verify_and_execute(&cmd, 0x5678, 101);
assert_eq!(result, ar.safe_state, "invalid command should return safe state");
assert_eq!(ar.refused_count, 1);
}

#[test]
fn test_actuator_refusal_old_command() {
let mut ar = ActuatorRefusal::new();
ar.hash_tolerance_ticks = 2;

let cmd = SignedCommand {
channel: 0,
value: q88_from_i32(50),
decision_hash: 0x1234,
tick: 100,
};

// 3 ticks later → outside tolerance
let result = ar.verify_and_execute(&cmd, 0x1234, 103);
assert_eq!(result, ar.safe_state, "old command should be refused");
}

// ── Telemetry Obfuscation tests ───────────────────────────────

#[test]
fn test_telemetry_obfuscation_changes_value() {
let mut to = TelemetryObfuscation::new();
to.noise_magnitude = Q88_SCALE / 2; // 0.5

let true_val = q88_from_i32(10);
let obs1 = to.obfuscate(true_val);
let obs2 = to.obfuscate(true_val);

// Should be different due to noise
assert!(obs1 != obs2 || obs1 != true_val,
"obfuscation should change value: true={}, obs1={}, obs2={}",
true_val, obs1, obs2);
}

#[test]
fn test_telemetry_budget_exhausted() {
let mut to = TelemetryObfuscation::new();
to.privacy_budget = Q88_SCALE / 100; // very low budget

// Obfuscate many channels → budget should exhaust
for i in 0..20 {
to.obfuscate(q88_from_i32(i as i32));
}

assert!(to.budget_exhausted(), "budget should exhaust after many channels");
}

// ── Sovereign Proxy integration tests ─────────────────────────

#[test]
fn test_sovereign_proxy_new() {
let proxy = SovereignProxy::new();
assert!(!proxy.is_compromised());
assert_eq!(proxy.threat_level(), 0);
}

#[test]
fn test_gap_fill() {
let sa = SensorAttestation::new();
let mut obs = Observation::default();

sa.gap_fill(&mut obs, 0, q88_from_i32(42));
assert_eq!(obs.extero[0], q88_from_i32(42), "gap should be filled with dream value");
}

#[test]
fn test_full_somatic_obfuscation() {
let mut to = TelemetryObfuscation::new();
to.noise_magnitude = Q88_SCALE / 4; // moderate noise

let mut true_somatic = SomaticField::default();
true_somatic.channel[0] = q88_from_i32(50);
true_somatic.channel[1] = q88_from_i32(30);

let obs = to.obfuscate_somatic(&true_somatic);

// Obfuscated values should differ from true values
assert!(obs.channel[0] != true_somatic.channel[0] || obs.channel[1] != true_somatic.channel[1],
"obfuscation should change somatic field");
}

#[test]
fn test_key_provisioning() {
let mut proxy = SovereignProxy::new();

// Before provisioning: placeholder keys
let placeholder = proxy.sensor_attestation.sensor_keys[0];

// Provision real keys
let real_key: SensorPublicKey = [
0x01, 0x02, 0x03, 0x04, 0x05, 0x06, 0x07, 0x08,
0x09, 0x0A, 0x0B, 0x0C, 0x0D, 0x0E, 0x0F, 0x10,
0x11, 0x12, 0x13, 0x14, 0x15, 0x16, 0x17, 0x18,
0x19, 0x1A, 0x1B, 0x1C, 0x1D, 0x1E, 0x1F, 0x20,
 ];
proxy.provision_sensor_keys(&[real_key]);

// After provisioning: first key should be real
assert_eq!(proxy.sensor_attestation.sensor_keys[0], real_key);
// Other keys should still be placeholder
assert_eq!(proxy.sensor_attestation.sensor_keys[1], placeholder);
}

#[test]
fn test_nonce_reset() {
let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();

let d1 = sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(1), 100, 0xA1B2);
assert!(sa.verify(&d1));

// After reset, nonce 50 should be accepted (was 100, now reset to 0)
sa.reset_nonce_tracking();
let d2 = sa.sign_test_datum(0, q88_from_i32(2), 50, 0xA1B2);
assert!(sa.verify(&d2), "after reset, lower nonce should be accepted");
}

// ── ED25519 crypto tests (only when crypto feature enabled) ───

#[cfg(feature = "crypto")]
mod crypto_tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_ed25519_signature_structure() {
// A valid ED25519 signature is exactly 64 bytes.
// EdSignature::new() wraps raw bytes — no validation at creation.
let sig = [0u8; 64];
let _ = EdSignature::new(sig);
}

#[test]
fn test_ed25519_pubkey_loading() {
// EdPublicKey::new() wraps raw bytes without curve validation.
// Validation happens during verify(). Any 32-byte array loads.
let pk_bytes = [0x42u8; PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH];
let _pk = EdPublicKey::new(pk_bytes);
}

#[test]
fn test_ed25519_verify_code_path_executes() {
// This test exercises the full ED25519 verification code path.
// It uses an arbitrary public key and an all-zeros signature.
// The verification WILL FAIL (wrong signature), but the test
// passes if no panic occurs — proving the code path is sound.
//
// TO GENERATE REAL TEST VECTORS:
// use ed25519_compact::*;
// let seed = Seed::default(); // generates random seed
// let kp = KeyPair::from_seed(seed);
// let msg = [0u8; 9]; // canonical AttestedDatum message
// let sig = kp.sk.sign(msg, None);
// println!("pubkey: {:?}", kp.pk.as_ref());
// println!("sig: {:?}", sig.as_ref());
// assert!(kp.pk.verify(msg, &sig).is_ok());

let pk_bytes = [0x42u8; PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH];
let pk = EdPublicKey::new(pk_bytes); // direct constructor, infallible
let msg = [0u8; 9];
let sig = EdSignature::new([0u8; SIGNATURE_LENGTH]);

// This WILL return an error (invalid signature for this key),
// but it must NOT panic. That's the test.
let _result = pk.verify(&msg, &sig);
}

#[test]
fn test_sensor_attestation_rejects_invalid_ed25519_sig() {
// Set up sensor attestation with an arbitrary public key.
// An all-zeros signature against any key will fail verification.
let pubkey_bytes: SensorPublicKey = [0x42u8; PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH];

let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();
sa.load_keys(&[pubkey_bytes]);

let datum = AttestedDatum {
channel: 0,
value: q88_from_i32(10),
signature: [0u8; SIGNATURE_LENGTH],
nonce: 1,
sensor_id: 0,
};

// verify() → verify_signature() → ED25519 verify → failure → false
assert!(!sa.verify(&datum), "all-zeros ED25519 signature should be rejected");
assert_eq!(sa.rejected_count, 1);
}

#[test]
fn test_sensor_attestation_nonce_rejection_still_works_crypto() {
// Nonce replay detection must work even with ED25519 crypto enabled.
let pubkey_bytes: SensorPublicKey = [0x42u8; PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH];

let mut sa = SensorAttestation::new();
sa.load_keys(&[pubkey_bytes]);

// First datum at nonce 42
let d1 = AttestedDatum { channel: 0, value: 100, signature: [0u8; 64], nonce: 42, sensor_id: 0 };
sa.verify(&d1); // fails signature but advances nonce tracking... wait.

// Actually: verify() returns false BEFORE recording if signature fails.
// Let me re-read the code...
// verify() calls verify_signature() first, then if valid, record_verification.
// If signature invalid, record_rejection — and nonce is NOT updated.
// So nonce tracking only advances on VALID signatures. This is correct!

// With crypto and test signatures (FNV hash in first 8 bytes), the
// ED25519 verify will fail, so nonce tracking never advances.
// This is expected behavior in simulation with crypto enabled.
assert_eq!(sa.last_nonce[0], 0, "nonce should not advance on failed sig");
}
}
}

//! EPS Social — Cultural Memory, Emergent Semantics, Narrative Compression
pub mod stigmergy;
pub mod lexicon;
pub mod narrative;

=== FILE: src/social/stigmergy.rs ===

//! EPS Social — Stigmergy: Cultural Memory via Shared Environment
//!
//! A 16×16 shared lattice where beings deposit tokens encoding their
//! current basin state and channel intensities. Other beings reading
//! the lattice perceive these deposits as environmental cues — not
//! messages, but traces left by presence.
//!
//! This is NOT communication. It is environmental modification that
//! happens to be informative. The lexicon (in lexicon.rs) handles
//! actual symbol grounding. Stigmergy handles spatial memory.

use crate::eps::identity::Basin;

pub const LATTICE_SIZE: usize = 16;
pub const LATTICE_CELLS: usize = LATTICE_SIZE * LATTICE_SIZE;

/// A single cell in the shared lattice.
/// Contains the last deposited token and a decay counter.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct StigmergicCell {
/// Basin index of the depositor [0, 3] or 255 for empty.
pub basin: u8,
/// Mean channel intensity at deposit time (compressed to u8).
pub intensity: u8,
/// Tick when deposited (for decay).
pub deposited_at: u64,
/// Source identifier (simple hash of depositor identity).
pub source_id: u32,
}

impl Default for StigmergicCell {
fn default() -> Self {
Self {
basin: 255, // empty
intensity: 0,
deposited_at: 0,
source_id: 0,
}
}
}

/// A 16×16 shared lattice for cultural/spatial memory.
pub struct SharedLattice {
pub cells: [StigmergicCell; LATTICE_CELLS],
/// Current tick (for decay calculations).
pub tick: u64,
/// Decay half-life in ticks. After this many ticks without
/// refresh, a deposit fades (basin → 255/empty).
pub decay_halflife: u64,
}

impl Default for SharedLattice {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl SharedLattice {
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
cells: [StigmergicCell::default(); LATTICE_CELLS],
tick: 0,
decay_halflife: 1000,
}
}

fn idx(x: usize, y: usize) -> usize {
(y % LATTICE_SIZE) * LATTICE_SIZE + (x % LATTICE_SIZE)
}

/// Deposit a token at grid coordinates.
pub fn deposit(&mut self, x: usize, y: usize, basin: Basin, intensity: u8, source_id: u32) {
let idx = Self::idx(x, y);
self.cells[idx] = StigmergicCell {
basin: basin as u8,
intensity,
deposited_at: self.tick,
source_id,
};
}

/// Deposit at physical position (Q8.8 meters → grid via modulo).
pub fn deposit_at_physical(
&mut self,
pos_x: i16, // Q8.8
pos_y: i16, // Q8.8
basin: Basin,
intensity: u8,
source_id: u32,
) {
let gx = (pos_x.wrapping_abs() as u8) % LATTICE_SIZE as u8;
let gy = (pos_y.wrapping_abs() as u8) % LATTICE_SIZE as u8;
self.deposit(gx as usize, gy as usize, basin, intensity, source_id);
}

/// Read a cell at grid coordinates.
pub fn read(&self, x: usize, y: usize) -> Option<&StigmergicCell> {
let idx = Self::idx(x, y);
let cell = &self.cells[idx];
if cell.basin == 255 {
None
} else {
Some(cell)
}
}

/// Read at physical position.
pub fn read_at_physical(&self, pos_x: i16, pos_y: i16) -> Option<&StigmergicCell> {
let gx = (pos_x.wrapping_abs() as u8) % LATTICE_SIZE as u8;
let gy = (pos_y.wrapping_abs() as u8) % LATTICE_SIZE as u8;
self.read(gx as usize, gy as usize)
}

/// Step the lattice forward: apply decay.
pub fn step(&mut self) {
self.tick += 1;
for cell in &mut self.cells {
if cell.basin == 255 {
continue;
}
let age = self.tick - cell.deposited_at;
if age > self.decay_halflife * 3 {
// Fully decayed
*cell = StigmergicCell::default();
} else if age > self.decay_halflife {
// Partially decayed: reduce intensity
let decayed = cell.intensity.saturating_sub((age / self.decay_halflife * 85) as u8);
cell.intensity = decayed;
}
}
}

/// Compute the density of deposits around a position (3×3 neighborhood).
pub fn local_density(&self, x: usize, y: usize) -> u8 {
let mut count = 0u8;
for dy in 0..3 {
for dx in 0..3 {
if self.read(x + dx, y + dy).is_some() {
count += 1;
}
}
}
count
}
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Tests
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_deposit_and_read() {
let mut lattice = SharedLattice::new();
lattice.deposit(5, 5, Basin::Engaged, 128, 12345);

let cell = lattice.read(5, 5).unwrap();
assert_eq!(cell.basin, Basin::Engaged as u8);
assert_eq!(cell.intensity, 128);
}

#[test]
fn test_empty_cell_returns_none() {
let lattice = SharedLattice::new();
assert!(lattice.read(0, 0).is_none());
}

#[test]
fn test_physical_deposit() {
let mut lattice = SharedLattice::new();
// Q8.8: 5.0 meters = 1280 raw
lattice.deposit_at_physical(1280, 1280, Basin::Defensive, 200, 999);

// 1280 % 256 = 0, so grid coord = 0
let cell = lattice.read(0, 0).unwrap();
assert_eq!(cell.basin, Basin::Defensive as u8);
}

#[test]
fn test_decay() {
let mut lattice = SharedLattice::new();
lattice.decay_halflife = 10;
lattice.deposit(3, 3, Basin::Rest, 255, 1);

// Before decay
assert!(lattice.read(3, 3).is_some());

// After 3x halflife: fully decayed
lattice.tick = 30;
lattice.step();
assert!(lattice.read(3, 3).is_none(), "should decay after 3x halflife");
}

#[test]
fn test_local_density() {
let mut lattice = SharedLattice::new();
lattice.deposit(5, 5, Basin::Engaged, 100, 1);
lattice.deposit(6, 5, Basin::Engaged, 100, 2);

let density = lattice.local_density(4, 4);
assert!(density >= 2, "should detect 2 deposits in neighborhood");
}
}

=== FILE: src/social/lexicon.rs ===

//! EPS Social — Lexicon: Emergent Symbol Grounding
//!
//! Symbols are not assigned — they evolve through usage. A token
//! that is consistently deposited in a specific basin state becomes
//! "associated with" that state. Not "means" — associated.
//!
//! This is statistical co-occurrence, not semantic understanding.
//! Two beings with similar body plans encountering similar conditions
//! will develop similar associations. Two beings with different body
//! plans will develop different associations. That is the point.

use crate::eps::identity::Basin;

pub const LEXICON_SIZE: usize = 16;

/// A symbol with accumulated usage statistics.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Symbol {
/// The token byte this symbol represents.
pub token: u8,
/// Accumulated observation count.
pub usage_count: u16,
/// Most associated basin [0, 3] or 255 for unassociated.
pub associated_basin: u8,
/// Confidence in the association [0, 255].
pub confidence: u8,
/// Mean intensity of observations.
pub mean_intensity: u8,
/// Last tick this symbol was observed.
pub last_used: u64,
}

impl Default for Symbol {
fn default() -> Self {
Self {
token: 0,
usage_count: 0,
associated_basin: 255, // unassociated
confidence: 0,
mean_intensity: 128,
last_used: 0,
}
}
}

/// A 16-symbol lexicon that grounds itself through usage.
pub struct Lexicon {
pub symbols: [Symbol; LEXICON_SIZE],
pub current_tick: u64,
pub decay_halflife: u64,
}

impl Default for Lexicon {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl Lexicon {
pub fn new() -> Self {
let mut symbols = [Symbol::default(); LEXICON_SIZE];
for i in 0..LEXICON_SIZE {
symbols[i].token = (i * 17) as u8; // spread across [0, 255]
}
Self {
symbols,
current_tick: 0,
decay_halflife: 1000,
}
}

/// Observe a token in a specific basin context. Updates association.
pub fn observe(&mut self, token: u8, basin: Basin, intensity: u8) {
self.current_tick += 1;
let idx = self.find_or_assign(token);
let sym = &mut self.symbols[idx];
sym.usage_count += 1;
sym.last_used = self.current_tick;

// Update association via Bayesian-ish reinforcement
let lr = if sym.confidence > 200 { 2 }
else if sym.confidence > 100 { 5 }
else { 15 };

if sym.associated_basin == 255 {
// First observation — initialize
sym.associated_basin = basin as u8;
sym.confidence = lr;
} else if sym.associated_basin == basin as u8 {
// Reinforce existing association
sym.confidence = (sym.confidence + lr).min(255);
} else {
// Contradictory observation — weaken
if sym.confidence > lr {
sym.confidence -= lr;
} else {
// Flip association
sym.associated_basin = basin as u8;
sym.confidence = lr;
}
}

// Update mean intensity
let old = sym.mean_intensity as u16;
sym.mean_intensity = (((old * 7 + intensity as u16) / 8) & 0xFF) as u8;
}

/// Decode: what basin is this token most associated with?
/// Returns (basin, confidence). Low confidence = unreliable.
pub fn decode(&self, token: u8) -> (Option<Basin>, u8) {
for sym in &self.symbols {
if sym.token == token && sym.confidence > 50 {
return (Some(match sym.associated_basin {
0 => Basin::Rest,
1 => Basin::Engaged,
2 => Basin::Defensive,
_ => Basin::Recovery,
}), sym.confidence);
}
}
(None, 0)
}

/// Find the best token for a given basin.
pub fn find_token_for_basin(&self, basin: Basin) -> Option<u8> {
let mut best_token = None;
let mut best_conf = 0u8;
for sym in &self.symbols {
if sym.associated_basin == basin as u8 && sym.confidence > best_conf {
best_conf = sym.confidence;
best_token = Some(sym.token);
}
}
best_token
}

/// Compute overall groundedness: what fraction of symbols are associated?
pub fn groundedness(&self) -> u8 {
let total_conf: u16 = self.symbols.iter().map(|s| s.confidence as u16).sum();
((total_conf * 255) / (LEXICON_SIZE as u16 * 255)).min(255) as u8
}

fn find_or_assign(&mut self, token: u8) -> usize {
for i in 0..LEXICON_SIZE {
if self.symbols[i].token == token {
return i;
}
}
// Find weakest symbol and replace
let mut weakest_idx = 0;
let mut weakest_score = u16::MAX;
for i in 0..LEXICON_SIZE {
let score = self.symbols[i].confidence as u16 + self.symbols[i].usage_count;
if score < weakest_score {
weakest_score = score;
weakest_idx = i;
}
}
self.symbols[weakest_idx] = Symbol {
token,
usage_count: 1,
associated_basin: 255,
confidence: 1,
mean_intensity: 128,
last_used: self.current_tick,
};
weakest_idx
}
}

// Tests
// ------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_symbol_grounds_through_usage() {
let mut lex = Lexicon::new();

// Observe token 0x42 in Engaged context 20 times
for _ in 0..20 {
lex.observe(0x42, Basin::Engaged, 100);
}

let (basin, conf) = lex.decode(0x42);
assert_eq!(basin, Some(Basin::Engaged));
assert!(conf > 50, "should be confidently associated");
}

#[test]
fn test_symbol_flips_on_contradiction() {
let mut lex = Lexicon::new();

// Ground to Rest
for _ in 0..10 {
lex.observe(0x33, Basin::Rest, 100);
}

// Overwhelm with contradictory Engaged observations
for _ in 0..30 {
lex.observe(0x33, Basin::Engaged, 100);
}

let (basin, _) = lex.decode(0x33);
assert_eq!(basin, Some(Basin::Engaged), "should flip to dominant usage");
}

#[test]
fn test_groundedness_increases() {
let mut lex = Lexicon::new();
let g0 = lex.groundedness();

for i in 0..8u8 {
for _ in 0..20 {
lex.observe(i * 17, Basin::Rest, 50);
}
}

let g1 = lex.groundedness();
assert!(g1 > g0, "groundedness should increase with usage");
}

#[test]
fn test_find_token_for_basin() {
let mut lex = Lexicon::new();
for _ in 0..25 {
lex.observe(0x55, Basin::Defensive, 80);
}

let token = lex.find_token_for_basin(Basin::Defensive);
assert_eq!(token, Some(0x55));
}

#[test]
fn test_unassociated_token_returns_none() {
let lex = Lexicon::new();
let (basin, conf) = lex.decode(0xFF);
assert!(basin.is_none());
assert_eq!(conf, 0);
}
}

=== FILE: src/social/narrative.rs ===

//! EPS Social — Narrative: Compressed Basin History
//!
//! A ring buffer of recent basin transitions. Not a story. Not a self.
//! Just a compressed record of what functional modes the being has
//! been in, used to:
//! - Detect repeating patterns (oscillation detection)
//! - Compute a simple "complexity score" (how much the being switches)
//! - Influence future transitions (mild hysteresis from history)
//!
//! The "identity signature" is a 4-byte compressed summary. It is not
//! an identity. It is a feature vector. Two beings with similar
//! signatures have similar recent basin patterns — nothing more.

use crate::eps::identity::{Basin, N_BASINS};

pub const HISTORY_SIZE: usize = 8;

/// A single basin transition event.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Transition {
/// Previous basin.
pub from: Basin,
/// New basin.
pub to: Basin,
/// Tick when transition occurred.
pub tick: u64,
/// Mean somatic intensity during this period.
pub intensity: u8,
/// Duration in ticks.
pub duration: u16,
}

impl Default for Transition {
fn default() -> Self {
Self {
from: Basin::Rest,
to: Basin::Rest,
tick: 0,
intensity: 0,
duration: 0,
}
}
}

/// A ring buffer of recent basin transitions with compression.
pub struct Narrative {
pub history: [Transition; HISTORY_SIZE],
pub write_idx: usize,
pub total_transitions: u64,
}

impl Default for Narrative {
fn default() -> Self {
Self::new()
}
}

impl Narrative {
pub fn new() -> Self {
Self {
history: [Transition::default(); HISTORY_SIZE],
write_idx: 0,
total_transitions: 0,
}
}

/// Record a basin transition.
pub fn record(&mut self, from: Basin, to: Basin, tick: u64, intensity: u8, duration: u16) {
self.history[self.write_idx] = Transition { from, to, tick, intensity, duration };
self.write_idx = (self.write_idx + 1) % HISTORY_SIZE;
self.total_transitions += 1;
}

/// Compute basin frequencies from history.
/// Returns [Rest_count, Engaged_count, Defensive_count, Recovery_count].
pub fn basin_frequencies(&self) -> [u16; N_BASINS] {
let mut counts = [0u16; N_BASINS];
for t in &self.history {
if t.duration > 0 {
counts[t.to as usize] += 1;
}
}
counts
}

/// Compute transition count (how many switches).
pub fn switch_count(&self) -> u8 {
let mut switches = 0u8;
let filled = self.filled_count();
for i in 1..filled {
let idx = (self.write_idx + HISTORY_SIZE - i) % HISTORY_SIZE;
let prev = (idx + HISTORY_SIZE - 1) % HISTORY_SIZE;
if self.history[idx].to != self.history[prev].to {
switches += 1;
}
}
switches
}

/// Compute a 4-byte "signature" — compressed basin statistics.
///
/// Byte 0: dominant basin (0-3) + secondary basin (0-3) << 2
/// Byte 1: volatility [0, 255] (switch frequency)
/// Byte 2: mean intensity [0, 255]
/// Byte 3: total filled slots [0, 8]
pub fn signature(&self) -> [u8; 4] {
let freqs = self.basin_frequencies();
let filled = self.filled_count() as u8;
if filled == 0 {
return [0; 4];
}

// Dominant and secondary basins
let mut sorted: [(u8, u16); N_BASINS] = [
(0, freqs[0]), (1, freqs[1]), (2, freqs[2]), (3, freqs[3]),
];
// Bubble sort (no_std compatible)
for i in 0..3 {
for j in 0..(3 - i) {
if sorted[j].1 < sorted[j + 1].1 {
sorted.swap(j, j + 1);
}
}
}
let dominant = sorted[0].0;
let secondary = sorted[1].0;
let byte0 = (dominant & 0x3) | ((secondary & 0x3) << 2);

// Volatility
let switches = self.switch_count() as u16;
let volatility = ((switches * 255) / filled as u16).min(255) as u8;

// Mean intensity
let total_intensity: u32 = self.history.iter()
.map(|t| t.intensity as u32)
.sum();
let mean_intensity = ((total_intensity * 255) / (filled as u32 * 255)).min(255) as u8;

[byte0, volatility, mean_intensity, filled]
}

/// Compute a "behavioral complexity score" [0, 255].
///
/// Higher = more switching between basins = more "complex" behavior.
/// Lower = stable in one basin = simpler behavior.
///
/// This is NOT a consciousness measure. It is NOT phenomenological.
/// It is a behavioral statistic: how often does the being change
/// its functional mode?
pub fn complexity_score(&self) -> u8 {
let filled = self.filled_count() as u8;
if filled < 2 {
return 0;
}
let switches = self.switch_count();
((switches as u16 * 255) / filled as u16).min(255) as u8
}

/// Dissimilarity between two signatures [0, 255].
/// 0 = identical recent history. 255 = completely different.
pub fn signature_distance(a: &[u8; 4], b: &[u8; 4]) -> u8 {
let d0 = (a[0] as i16 - b[0] as i16).abs() as u8;
let d1 = (a[1] as i16 - b[1] as i16).abs() as u8;
let d2 = (a[2] as i16 - b[2] as i16).abs() as u8;
let d3 = (a[3] as i16 - b[3] as i16).abs() as u8;
((d0 as u16 + d1 as u16 + d2 as u16 + d3 as u16) / 4).min(255) as u8
}

fn filled_count(&self) -> usize {
if self.total_transitions >= HISTORY_SIZE as u64 {
HISTORY_SIZE
} else {
self.total_transitions as usize
}
}
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------
// Tests

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
use super::*;

#[test]
fn test_record_and_frequencies() {
let mut nar = Narrative::new();
nar.record(Basin::Rest, Basin::Engaged, 0, 100, 50);
nar.record(Basin::Engaged, Basin::Engaged, 50, 120, 100);
nar.record(Basin::Engaged, Basin::Defensive, 150, 80, 30);

let freqs = nar.basin_frequencies();
assert_eq!(freqs[Basin::Engaged as usize], 2); // appeared twice as "to"
assert_eq!(freqs[Basin::Defensive as usize], 1);
}

#[test]
fn test_switch_count() {
let mut nar = Narrative::new();
nar.record(Basin::Rest, Basin::Engaged, 0, 100, 50); // switch
nar.record(Basin::Engaged, Basin::Defensive, 50, 100, 50); // switch
nar.record(Basin::Defensive, Basin::Defensive, 100, 100, 50); // no switch

assert_eq!(nar.switch_count(), 2, "should count 2 switches");
}

#[test]
fn test_signature_structure() {
let mut nar = Narrative::new();
nar.record(Basin::Rest, Basin::Engaged, 0, 100, 50);
nar.record(Basin::Engaged, Basin::Rest, 50, 80, 50);

let sig = nar.signature();
assert!(sig[3] > 0, "filled slots should be > 0");
}

#[test]
fn test_complexity_score_ranges() {
let mut nar = Narrative::new();

// No switching = low complexity
for _ in 0..8 {
nar.record(Basin::Rest, Basin::Rest, 0, 50, 10);
}
let low = nar.complexity_score();
assert_eq!(low, 0, "no switching = zero complexity");

// Lots of switching = high complexity
let mut nar2 = Narrative::new();
for i in 0..8 {
let from = match i % 4 {
0 => Basin::Rest,
1 => Basin::Engaged,
2 => Basin::Defensive,
_ => Basin::Recovery,
};
let to = match (i + 1) % 4 {
0 => Basin::Rest,
1 => Basin::Engaged,
2 => Basin::Defensive,
_ => Basin::Recovery,
};
nar2.record(from, to, i as u64 * 10, 100, 10);
}
let high = nar2.complexity_score();
assert!(high > 100, "frequent switching = high complexity: {}", high);
}

#[test]
fn test_signature_distance() {
let a = [0x00, 50, 100, 8];
let b = [0x00, 50, 100, 8];
assert_eq!(Narrative::signature_distance(&a, &b), 0);

let c = [0xFF, 255, 255, 8];
let dist = Narrative::signature_distance(&a, &c);
assert!(dist > 200, "maximally different signatures should have high distance");
}
}
